\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}),\bm{\zeta}\big)-\big((\bm{v}^{k}\otimes(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}+\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{k}),\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big(2\nu(\varphi^{k})D\bm{v}^{k},D\bm{\zeta}\big)+\gamma\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{k},\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\big((\mu^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}^{k}\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)

(3.30)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big),\quad\forall\,\bm{\zeta}\in\bm{Y}_{k},

(3.31)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\varphi^{k},\xi)+(\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k},\xi)+\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k},\nabla\xi\big)=0,\quad\forall\,\xi\in Z_{k},

(3.32)

\displaystyle(\mu^{k},\xi)=\big(-\Delta\varphi^{k}+\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k},\xi\big),\quad\forall\,\xi\in Z_{k},

in
(0,T)
,
where

(3.33)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{k}=-\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k},\quad\widehat{R}^{k}=-m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\cdot\nabla\mu^{k},\quad\text{in}\ \Omega\times(0,T).

The (finite dimensional) solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k})

is subject to the boundary and initial conditions

(3.34)

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{k}=\bm{0},\quad\partial_{\bm{n}}\varphi^{k}=\partial_{\bm{n}}\mu^{k}=0,

\displaystyle\quad\textrm{on}\ \partial\Omega\times(0,T),

(3.35)

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{k}|_{t=0}=\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{v}_{0},\quad\varphi^{k}|_{t=0}=\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n},

\displaystyle\quad\text{in}\ \Omega.

Then we have the following lemma, whose proof is sketched in the Appendix.

Lemma 3.2
.

Given
\sigma^{k}=\mathbf{F}^{k}_{1}(\bm{u}^{k},\psi^{k})
, the Faedo–Galerkin scheme (
3.30
)–(
3.35
) admits a unique solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k})
on
[0,T]
such that

\bm{v}^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k}),\quad\varphi^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T];Z_{k}),\quad\mu^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T];Z_{k}).

Moreover,
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
is bounded in
H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k})
.

Thanks to Lemma
3.2
, we can define the following mapping, which is determined by the unique solution to problem (
3.30
)–(
3.35
):

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{k}_{2}:\quad\widehat{X}

\displaystyle\to\ H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k}),

\displaystyle\sigma^{k}

\displaystyle\mapsto\ (\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k}).

Furthermore,
\mathbf{F}^{k}_{2}
is a bounded operator from
\widehat{X}
to
H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k})
.

Next, we verify the continuity of
\mathbf{F}^{k}_{2}
. Given
\sigma^{k}_{i}\in\widehat{X}
,
i=1,2
, we define the corresponding solutions
(\bm{v}^{k}_{i},\varphi^{k}_{i})=\mathbf{F}^{k}_{2}(\sigma^{k}_{i})
,
i=1,2
, determined by Lemma
3.2
(with the same initial data
(\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{v}_{0},\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n})
).
The associated chemical potentials are denoted by
\mu_{i}^{k}
,
i=1,2
, respectively. As before, we denote the differences

\Sigma^{k}=\sigma_{1}^{k}-\sigma_{2}^{k},\quad\bm{V}^{k}=\bm{v}^{k}_{1}-\bm{v}^{k}_{2},\quad\Phi^{k}=\varphi_{1}^{k}-\varphi_{2}^{k},\quad\Upsilon^{k}=\mu_{1}^{k}-\mu_{2}^{k}.

We first observe that for
i=1,2
, (
3.30
) can be rewritten as (cf. (
3.21
))

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{i}^{k})\partial_{t}\bm{v_{i}}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big(\big((\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{i}^{k})\bm{v}_{i}^{k}+\widehat{\mathbf{J}}_{i}^{k})\cdot\nabla\big)\bm{v}_{i}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big(2\nu(\varphi_{i}^{k})D\bm{v}_{i}^{k},D\bm{\zeta}\big)+\gamma\big(|\nabla\bm{v}_{i}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}_{i}^{k},\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\big((\mu_{i}^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{i}^{k})\sigma_{i}^{k})\nabla\varphi_{i}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}_{i}^{k}\bm{v}_{i}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)

(3.36)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{i}^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}_{i}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi_{i}^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi_{i}^{k})\nabla\mu_{i}^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}_{i}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big),\quad\forall\,\bm{\zeta}\in\bm{Y}_{k},

Taking the difference of (
3.30
) for
\bm{v}^{k}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, and testing the resultant by
\bm{\zeta}=\bm{V}^{k}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\big(2\nu(\varphi^{k}_{1})D\bm{V}^{k},D\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\gamma\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{1}|^{2}\nabla\bm{V}^{k},\nabla\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=-\big((\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{2}^{k}))\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)-\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})(\bm{V}^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big((\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{2}^{k}))(\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)-\big(2(\nu(\varphi^{k}_{1})-\nu(\varphi^{k}_{2}))D\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\nabla\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\gamma\big((|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{1}|^{2}-|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2}|^{2})\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2},\nabla\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})m(\varphi_{1}^{k})(\nabla\Upsilon^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big((\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})m(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}^{k})m(\varphi_{2}^{k}))(\nabla\mu_{2}^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(\mu_{1}^{k}\nabla\Phi^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\big(\Upsilon^{k}\nabla\varphi_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)-\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\sigma_{1}^{k}\nabla\Phi^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big((\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\sigma_{1}^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}^{k})\sigma_{2}^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k}_{2},\bm{V}^{k}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\big((m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\cdot\nabla\Upsilon^{k})\bm{v}^{k}_{2},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\big(((m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})-m(\varphi^{k}_{2})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{2}))\cdot\nabla\mu^{k}_{2})\bm{v}^{k}_{2},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}_{1}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{1})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\mu^{k}_{1}\big)\big)\bm{v}_{2}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left.\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{2})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{2})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{2})\nabla\mu^{k}_{2}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k}_{2},\bm{V}^{k}\big)\right)

(3.37)

\displaystyle\quad=:\sum_{j=4}^{17}I_{j},

where we have used integration by parts and the following identity (cf. (
3.21
) for
\varphi_{1}^{k}
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\partial_{t}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)-\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})(\bm{v}_{1}^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})m(\varphi_{1}^{k})(\nabla\mu_{1}^{k}\cdot\nabla)\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2}\big((m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\cdot\nabla\mu^{k}_{1})\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}_{1}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{1})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\mu^{k}_{1}\big)\big)\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big).

Let us estimate the right-hand side of (
3.37
) term by term. Using Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle|I_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{2}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{4}\|D\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}\leq C\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}\leq C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{6}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{2}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{4}\|D\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{4}\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{7}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\nu(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\nu(\varphi_{2}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|D\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\nabla\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|D\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|D\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{4}\|D\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{8}|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\big(\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{1}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\big)\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\big(\|\bm{v}^{k}_{1}\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\big)\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}.

To proceed, we need some estimates for the chemical potential.
Taking
\xi=\mu_{1}^{k}
in (
3.32
) for
\mu_{1}^{k}
, we find

\displaystyle\|\mu_{1}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta\varphi_{1}^{k}\|\|\mu_{1}^{k}\|+\left(\|\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\|+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|\right)\|\mu_{1}^{k}\|.

Since for any given
k
there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}_{k}>0
such that
\|\varphi_{i}^{k}\|_{C([0,T];H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq\widetilde{C}_{k}
,
i=1,2
, it follows from the Sobolev embedding theorem and the construction of
\Psi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}
that

(3.38)

\|\mu_{1}^{k}\|\leq C_{k}+C\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|,

where the positive constant
C_{k}
depends on
k
and
\epsilon
.
The same result holds for
\mu_{2}^{k}
. Next, taking
\xi=\Upsilon^{k}
in (
3.32
) for the difference
\Upsilon^{k}
, we easily obtain

\displaystyle\|\Upsilon^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\|\Delta\Phi^{k}\|+\|\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi_{2}^{k})\|\big)\|\Upsilon^{k}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\Sigma^{k}\|+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|\big)\|\Upsilon^{k}\|,

which entails

(3.39)

\|\Upsilon^{k}\|\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|)\|\Phi^{k}\|+C\|\Sigma^{k}\|.

As a consequence, we can deduce that

\displaystyle|I_{9}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})m(\varphi_{1}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Upsilon^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\big((1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|)\|\Phi^{k}\|+\|\Sigma^{k}\|\big)\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|^{2})(\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2})+\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{10}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})m(\varphi_{1}^{k})-\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}^{k})m(\varphi_{2}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu_{2}^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|(1+\|\sigma_{2}^{k}\|)\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|^{2})\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{11}|+|I_{12}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{1}^{k}\|\|\nabla\Phi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}+\|\Upsilon^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi_{2}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|)\|\Phi^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|+C_{k}\big((1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|)\|\Phi^{k}\|+\|\Sigma^{k}\|\big)\|\varphi_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{1}\|^{2}+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|^{2})\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+C(1+\|\varphi^{k}_{2}\|^{2})\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+C\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{13}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|\|\nabla\Phi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|\|\Phi^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\sigma_{1}^{k}\|^{2}\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{14}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\sigma_{1}^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}^{k})\sigma_{2}^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(\|\Sigma^{k}\|+\|\sigma_{2}^{k}\|\|\Phi^{k}\|)\|\varphi_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\varphi_{2}^{k}\|^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\sigma_{2}^{k}\|^{2}\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{15}|

\displaystyle\leq\|m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Upsilon^{k}\|\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\big((1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|)\|\Phi^{k}\|+\|\Sigma^{k}\|\big)\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|^{2})\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

\displaystyle|I_{16}|

\displaystyle\leq\|m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})-m(\varphi^{k}_{2})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|(1+\|\sigma_{2}^{k}\|)\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}_{2}\|^{2})\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|^{2}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2},

and finally, some calculations in a similar fashion yield

\displaystyle|I_{17}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\big\|(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}_{1}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{1})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\mu^{k}_{1}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\quad-(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{2})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{2})\nabla\mu^{k}_{2}\big)\big)\big\|\|\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{1})-\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k}_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}_{2})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{2})\nabla\mu^{k}_{2}\big)\big)\|\|\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{6}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\big(\|\bm{v}^{k}_{1}\|\|\Phi^{k}\|+\|\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}_{2}\|+\|\varphi^{k}_{1}\|\|\Upsilon^{k}\|+\|\Phi^{k}\|\|\mu^{k}_{2}\|+\|\Phi^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}_{2}\|\|\mu^{k}_{2}\|\big)\|\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\quad+C_{k}(\|\Upsilon^{k}\|+\|\Phi^{k}\|\|\mu^{k}_{2}\|)\|\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|+C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|\big(\|\bm{v}^{k}_{2}\|\|\varphi^{k}_{2}\|+\|\varphi^{k}_{2}\|\|\mu_{2}^{k}\|+\|\mu^{k}_{2}\|\big)\|\bm{v}_{2}^{k}\|\|\bm{V}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}(\|\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}+\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2}\big).

In the above estimates, we have essentially used the fact that
\bm{v}^{k}
,
\varphi^{k}
and
\mu^{k}
are finite dimensional. Hence, all related estimates for higher-order norms depend on the parameter
k
at this stage.

Next, taking the difference of (
3.31
) for
\varphi^{k}_{i}
, we see that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\Phi^{k},\xi)

\displaystyle=-(\Phi^{k}\bm{v}^{k}_{1},\nabla\xi)-(\varphi^{k}_{2}\bm{V}^{k},\nabla\xi)-\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\Upsilon^{k},\nabla\xi\big)

(3.40)

\displaystyle\quad-\big((m(\varphi^{k}_{1})-m(\varphi^{k}_{2}))\nabla\mu^{k}_{2},\nabla\xi\big),\quad\forall\,\xi\in Z_{k}.

Since
\overline{\Phi^{k}}=0
, we can take
\xi=\mathcal{N}\Phi^{k}
in (
3.40
) and obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\big(\Phi^{k}\bm{v}^{k}_{1},\nabla\mathcal{N}\Phi^{k}\big)-\big(\varphi^{k}_{2}\bm{V}^{k},\nabla\mathcal{N}\Phi^{k}\big)-\big(m(\varphi^{k}_{1})\nabla\Upsilon^{k},\nabla\mathcal{N}\Phi^{k}\big)

(3.41)

\displaystyle\quad-\big((m(\varphi^{k}_{1})-m(\varphi^{k}_{2}))\nabla\mu^{k}_{2},\nabla\mathcal{N}\Phi^{k}\big).

The terms on the right-hand side of (
3.41
) can be estimated in a similar manner as above, so we get

(3.42)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}+C_{k}\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\nu_{*}}{4}\|D\bm{V}^{k}\|^{2}.

Furthermore, the interpolation inequality (
2.1
) yields

\displaystyle\|\Phi^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}.

Collecting the above estimates, we can deduce from (
3.37
) and (
3.42
) that

(3.43)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{C_{k}}{\rho_{*}}\left(\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\bm{V}^{k},\bm{V}^{k}\big)+\|\Phi^{k}\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}\right)+C_{k}\|\Sigma^{k}\|^{2}.

Applying Gronwall’s lemma to (
3.43
), and recalling that
\bm{V}^{k}|_{t=0}=\bm{0}
,
\Phi^{k}|_{t=0}=0
, we obtain

(3.44)

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k}(t))\bm{V}^{k}(t),\bm{V}^{k}(t)\big)+\|\Phi^{k}(t)\|_{V_{0}^{-1}}^{2}\leq C_{k}e^{C_{k}t}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Sigma^{k}(s)\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

Since both
\bm{V}^{k}
and
\Xi^{k}
are finite dimensional and
\widehat{\rho}(\varphi_{1}^{k})\geq\rho_{*}
, we conclude from (
3.44
) that the solution operator
\mathbf{F}^{k}_{2}
is continuous as a mapping from
X
to
C([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C([0,T];Z_{k})
.

Step 3.
Define the composite mapping
\mathbf{F}^{k}:=\mathbf{F}_{2}^{k}\circ\mathbf{F}_{1}^{k}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{k}:\ \ C^{\delta}([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T];Z_{k})\

\displaystyle\ \to H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k}),

\displaystyle(\bm{u}^{k},\psi^{k})\

\displaystyle\ \mapsto(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k}).

From the compactness of
H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})
in

C^{\delta}([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k})
and
H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k})
in

C^{\delta}([0,T];Z_{k})
(recalling that
\bm{Y}_{k}
and
Z_{k}
are finite-dimensional), we find that
\mathbf{F}^{k}
is a compact operator from
C^{\delta}([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T];Z_{k})
into itself.
On the other hand, it follows from the continuous dependence estimates (
3.29
) and (
3.44
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{v}_{1}^{k}(t)-\bm{v}^{k}_{2}(t)\|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\varphi_{1}^{k}(t)-\varphi^{k}_{2}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bm{u}_{1}^{k}(t)-\bm{u}_{2}^{k}(t)\|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\psi_{1}^{k}(t)-\psi_{2}^{k}(t)\|\right).

Due to the boundedness of
(\bm{v}^{k}_{i},\varphi^{k}_{i})
in
H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k})
,
i=1,2
, we can conclude by interpolation that
\mathbf{F}^{k}
is a continuous operator from
C^{\delta}([0,T];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T];Z_{k})
into itself.

Take

\widetilde{M}=2\mathrm{e}(C_{0}+1)+2C_{0,k}+2(\|\bm{v}_{0}\|^{2}+\|\varphi_{0,n}\|^{2}+1),

where the positive constants
C_{0}
,
C_{0,k}
are given in (
A.6
) and (
A.7
), respectively.
According to Lemma
3.1
and estimate (
A.8
), there exists a sufficiently small time
T_{*}\in(0,T]
depending on
\widetilde{M}
such that

\|\bm{v}^{k}(t)\|^{2}+\|\varphi^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq 2\mathrm{e}(C_{0}+1)+2C_{0,k}<\widetilde{M},\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{*}].

Define

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{k}

\displaystyle=\Big\{(\bm{u}^{k},\psi^{k})\in C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];Z_{k})\ \Big|\ \sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\|\bm{u}^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{M},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\|\psi^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{M},\ \bm{u}^{k}(0)=\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{v}_{0},\ \psi^{k}(0)=\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n}\Big\},

which is a closed convex set in
C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];Z_{k})
. Then for any
(\bm{u}^{k},\psi^{k})\in\bm{B}_{k}
, we find that

(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})=\mathbf{F}^{k}(\bm{u}^{k},\psi^{k})\in H^{1}(0,T_{*};\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T_{*};Z_{k})\subset\subset C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];\bm{Y}_{k})\times C^{\delta}([0,T_{*}];Z_{k}),

and the pair
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\|\bm{v}^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{M},\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\|\varphi^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{M}.

As a result, it holds
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})\in\bm{B}_{k}
.

We now recall the classical Schauder fixed-point theorem:

Lemma 3.3
.

Assume that
\bm{K}
is a closed convex set in a Banach space

\mathcal{B}
. Let
\mathcal{T}
be a continuous mapping of
\bm{K}
into itself that satisfies that the image
\mathcal{T}\bm{K}
is precompact.
Then there exists a fixed point in
\bm{K}
for
\mathcal{T}
.

Applying Lemma
3.3
, we can conclude that for the small time
T_{*}\in(0,T]
chosen above, the mapping
\mathbf{F}^{k}
admits a fixed point
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
in the set
\bm{B}_{k}
. Then
\sigma^{k}
can be determined by
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
as in Lemma
3.1
. Subsequently,
\mu^{k}
is determined by (
3.16
).
This gives a local solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})
to the semi-Galerkin scheme (
3.14
)–(
3.20
) in the interval
[0,T_{*}]
. The uniqueness of the approximate solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})
can be established by the standard energy method, using the facts that
\bm{v}^{k}
,
\varphi^{k}
,
\mu^{k}
are finite dimensional and
\sigma^{k}
is sufficiently smooth. Thus, we omit the details here.

The proof of Proposition
3.1
is complete.
∎

4.
Existence of Global Finite Energy/Weak Solutions

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.1
and Corollary
2.1
on the existence of a global finite energy/weak solution.

4.1.
Uniform estimates

We derive several estimates for the approximate solutions
\left\{(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})\right\}
to the semi-Galerkin scheme (
3.14
)–(
3.20
) obtained in Proposition
3.1
, which are uniform with respect to the approximating parameters
k
,
\epsilon
,
\gamma
,
n
and
t\in[0,T]
.

Step 1. Conservation of mass.
Taking
\xi=1
in (
3.15
), we obtain

(4.1)

\overline{\varphi^{k}}(t)=\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\int_{\Omega}\varphi^{k}(x,t)\,{\rm d}x=\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\int_{\Omega}\varphi_{0,n}(x)\,{\rm d}x=\overline{\varphi_{0,n}},\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{k}].

Similarly, multiplying (
3.18
) by
1
and integrating over
\Omega
, we get

(4.2)

\|\sigma^{k}(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}=\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}(x,t)\,{\rm d}x=\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{0,n}(x)\,{\rm d}x=\|\sigma_{0,n}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)},\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{k}].

Step 2. Estimates from the basic energy law.

Thanks to Proposition
3.1
, we are allowed to take
\bm{\zeta}=\bm{v}^{k}
in (
3.14
). Then, using the modified mass balance equation (
3.21
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})|\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi^{k})|D\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\gamma\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{4}\,{\rm d}x

(4.3)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}(\mu^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k}\cdot\bm{v}^{k}\,{\rm d}x.

Next, choosing
\xi=\mu^{k}
in (
3.15
) and
\xi=\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}
in (
3.16
), respectively, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}+\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\right)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\partial_{t}\beta(\varphi^{k})\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})\mu^{k}\,{\rm d}x

(4.4)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\mu^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x=0.

Finally, testing (
3.18
) by
\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k})
, using integration by parts and the fact
\mathrm{div}\,\bm{v}^{k}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}(\ln\sigma^{k}-1)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}\sigma^{k})\beta(\varphi^{k})\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\,{\rm d}x

(4.5)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\,{\rm d}x=0.

Adding (
4.3
)–(
4.5
) together, we obtain the following energy identity

(4.6)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)+\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{k}(t)=0,\quad\text{for a.e.}\ t\in(0,T_{k}),

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})|\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}+\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})+\sigma^{k}(\ln\sigma^{k}-1)+\beta(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{k}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(2\nu(\varphi^{k})|D\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}+\gamma|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{4}+m(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\mu^{k}|^{2}+\sigma^{k}|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Using (H4) and taking
a=2\beta^{*}
,
b=\sigma^{k}-1
in Lemma
2.2
, we observe that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\beta(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\beta^{*}\int_{\{0\leq\sigma^{k}\leq 1\}}\sigma^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x+\beta^{*}\int_{\{\sigma^{k}\geq 1\}}1\,\mathrm{d}x+\beta^{*}\int_{\{\sigma^{k}\geq 1\}}(\sigma^{k}-1)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{*}|\Omega|+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{\sigma^{k}\geq 1\}}\left(e^{2\beta^{*}}-2\beta^{*}-1+\sigma^{k}(\ln\sigma^{k}-1)+1\right)\mathrm{d}x

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{*}|\Omega|+\frac{1}{2}e^{2\beta^{*}}|\Omega|+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\{\sigma^{k}\geq 1\}}\sigma^{k}\ln\sigma^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Then from (H4), the construction of the regularized initial data and (
4.7
) (applied to
\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n}
and
\sigma_{0,n}
), we can control the initial approximate energy as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(0)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\rho}(\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n})|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{v}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n}|^{2}+\Psi_{\epsilon}(\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n})\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\big(\sigma_{0,n}(\ln\sigma_{0,n}-1)+\beta(\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,n})\sigma_{0,n}\big)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{*}\|\bm{v}_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\varphi_{0,n}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+|\Omega|\max_{r\in[-1,1]}|\Psi_{0}(r)|+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{0}\ln\sigma_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x+C(\beta^{*})|\Omega|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\rho^{*},\beta^{*},\|\bm{v}_{0}\|,\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)},\max_{r\in[-1,1]}|\Psi_{0}(r)|,\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{0}\ln\sigma_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x,\Omega\Big)

(4.8)

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{E}_{0},

where the upper bound
\mathcal{E}_{0}
is independent of the approximating parameters
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
.
On the other hand, from (H1), the construction of
\Psi_{\epsilon}
and (
4.7
), we also find that the total energy
\mathcal{E}^{k}(t)
is uniformly semi-coercive from below, that is,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\rho_{*}|\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}+|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}+\sigma^{k}\ln\sigma^{k}\right)(t)\,\mathrm{d}x-C_{*},

where the constant
C_{*}>0
is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
and time
t
.

Integrating (
4.6
) with respect to time, using (
4.8
), (
4.9
), we can deduce the following uniform bounds

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{k};\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{k};H^{1}(\Omega))}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\sigma^{k}\ln\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{k};L^{1}(\Omega))}+\|\Psi_{0,\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{k};L^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,

and

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{k};\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega))}+\gamma^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|_{L^{4}(0,T_{k};\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega))}+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{k};\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\quad+\big\|(\sigma^{k})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))\big\|_{L^{2}(0,T_{k};\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where the constant
C>0
depends on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
and coefficients of the system, but it is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
and time
t
.

Remark 4.1
.

For any
k\geq\widehat{k}
, the estimates (
4.10
), (
4.11
) allow us to extend the (unique) local solution

(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})

from
[0,T_{k}]
to the whole interval
[0,\infty)
. This yields a unique global solution at the approximate level. Moreover, the estimates (
4.10
), (
4.11
) hold on
[0,\infty)
, and the constant
C>0
is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
as well as time
t
.

Step 3. Estimate of the chemical potential
\mu^{k}
.

Thanks to Remark
4.1
, hereafter we shall work with the global approximate solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})
that is defined in
[0,\infty)
.

The construction of
\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}
implies that
\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)=\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(r)
for
r\in[-1+\epsilon,1-\epsilon]
. Hence, thanks to
[
58
, Proposition A.1]
and the mass conservation property (
4.1
), we deduce that

|\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)|\leq c_{1}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)\big(r-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\big)+c_{2},\qquad\forall\,r\in[-1+\epsilon,\,1-\epsilon],

where the positive constants
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
depend on
\overline{\varphi_{0,n}}
(and indeed only on
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
since
\overline{\varphi_{0,n}}\in\big[-|\overline{\varphi_{0}}|,\,|\overline{\varphi_{0}}|\big]
), but not on
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
.
In addition, we have

\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)\big(r-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\big)\geq\left(1-\epsilon-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\right)\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)\geq\frac{1}{2}(1-|\overline{\varphi_{0,n}}|)\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r),\quad\forall\,r\geq 1-\epsilon,

and

\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)\big(r-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\big)\geq\left(-1+\epsilon-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\right)\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r)\geq-\frac{1}{2}(1-|\overline{\varphi_{0,n}}|)\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(r),\quad\forall\,r\leq-1+\epsilon.

Thus, there exist two positive constants
\widetilde{c}_{1}
,
\widetilde{c}_{2}
depending on
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
, but not on
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
such that

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\widetilde{c}_{1}\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\big(\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x+\widetilde{c}_{2}.

Next, we apply Lemma
2.4
(with
\eta=1
) together with the estimates (
4.2
), (
4.10
) to conclude

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\varphi^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\big(\ln\sigma^{k}-\ln\overline{\sigma_{0,n}}\big)\mathrm{d}x+\|\sigma_{0,n}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+M\|\sigma_{0,n}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+M\|\sigma_{0,n}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\leq C,

where the constant
C>0
depends on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
, but not on
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
.
Taking
\xi=\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}}
in (
3.16
), we find that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}\big(\mu^{k}-\overline{\mu^{k}}\big)\big(\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x+\theta_{0}\|\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}}\|^{2}-\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}(\varphi^{k}-\overline{\varphi^{k}})\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.14)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|+C|\theta_{0}|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+C,

where in the last line we have used (H4), (
4.13
), and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality. On the other hand, testing (
3.16
) by
\xi=1
yields

\displaystyle|\overline{\mu^{k}}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\left|\int_{\Omega}\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+C\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}(\ln\sigma^{k}-1)\,\mathrm{d}x+C.

Then from (
4.10
)–(
4.15
) and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, we can conclude
that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;L^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,

and

(4.17)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\mu^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
.

Step 4. Sobolev estimates of
(\varphi^{k},\sigma^{k})
, Part I.

A direct calculation yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\big|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\sigma^{k}|^{2}}{\sigma^{k}}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}4\big|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}2\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\,\Delta\varphi^{k}\,{\rm d}x

(4.18)

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\Omega}2\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x.

We now estimate the last two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.18
). Using (H4), (
4.10
), the Sobolev embedding theorem and the elliptic regularity theory, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}2\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\,\Delta\varphi^{k}\,{\rm d}x\right|+\left|\int_{\Omega}2\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|+2\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\sigma^{k}\|(\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|+\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)})

(4.19)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|.

Testing (
3.16
) by
\xi=-\Delta\varphi^{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mu^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}\,{\rm d}x+\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\Delta\varphi^{k}\,{\rm d}x

(4.20)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|+|\theta_{0}|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2},

which together with (
4.10
) yields

(4.21)

\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|+\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\right).

Combining (
4.18
), (
4.19
) and (
4.21
), we end up with

(4.22)

\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}\big|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\big|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}+\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\right).\end{split}

Now, we are left to control the crucial term
\|\sigma^{k}\|
. To this end, an application of Lemma
2.3
with
u=\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}
,
q=4
,
r=2
,
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
leads to

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}=\big\|\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\big\|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{2}\big\|\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\ln^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{2}+C\big\|\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{4}+C_{\eta}

\displaystyle=\eta\big\|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{2}\int_{\Omega}\big|\sigma^{k}\ln\sigma^{k}\big|\,{\rm d}x+C\left(\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\,{\rm d}x\right)^{2}+C_{\eta}.

Thus, in light of (
4.2
) and (
4.10
), we infer from the above inequality that

(4.23)

\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq C\eta\big\|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{2}+C_{\eta},

where
C>0
is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
and
t
. Choosing
\eta
sufficiently small in (
4.23
), and using (
4.22
), we arrive at

(4.24)

\begin{split}\big\|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|^{2}\leq\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\big|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+C\big(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}\big).\end{split}

Owing to (
4.11
), an integration of (
4.24
) in time yields

(4.25)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\big\|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where
C>0
is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
and
t
.
This enables us to deduce from (
4.23
) that

(4.26)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C.

The above estimate combined with (
4.10
) and (
4.21
) further entails that

(4.27)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C.

Finally, we can apply (
4.10
) and the growth property of
\Psi_{\epsilon}
to conclude that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\Psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where
C>0
depends on
\epsilon
,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
and the coefficients of the system, but it is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
n
.

Step 5. Sobolev estimates of
(\varphi^{k},\sigma^{k})
, Part II.

We proceed to derive refined estimates with the help of additional information from
\|\sigma_{0,n}\|
. Multiplying (
3.18
) by
\sigma^{k}
and integrating over
\Omega
, after integration by parts, we get

(4.29)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}=-\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k}\cdot\nabla\sigma^{k}\,{\rm d}x.

It follows from Hölder’s inequality, the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality, and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left|-\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k}\cdot\nabla\sigma^{k}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sigma^{k}\|+\|\sigma^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C\big(1+\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}\big)\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C\big(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|)\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{4},

where we have used (H4), (
4.10
), (
4.21
). Hence, from (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) we infer that

(4.31)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq C\big(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\big)\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{4}.

It remains to control the right-hand side of (
4.31
).
Applying Lemma
2.3
to
\sigma^{k}
with the choice of parameters
q=2
,
r=1
,
\alpha=1
and using (
4.2
), (
4.10
), we obtain

(4.32)

\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq\eta\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|\|\sigma^{k}\ln\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+C_{\eta}.

Choosing
\eta>0
in (
4.32
) sufficiently small (
\eta
is independent of the approximating parameters and time
t
), and inserting the resultant into (
4.31
), we deduce from Young’s inequality that

(4.33)

\begin{split}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}\right)\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C.\end{split}

Recalling the following interpolation inequality

(4.34)

\displaystyle\|u\|\leq C\|u\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u\|^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\|u\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)},\quad\forall\,u\in H^{1}(\Omega),

applying (
4.2
) and Young’s inequality, we then arrive at

(4.35)

\begin{split}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C,\end{split}

where the constant
C>0
only depends on the parameters of the system,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
\Omega
.
Thanks to the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality and (
4.2
), we can rewrite (
4.35
) as follows

(4.36)

\begin{split}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+\left(\varpi-C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}\right)\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}\leq C,\end{split}

where the constant
\varpi>0
only depends on
\Omega
.
An application of Gronwall’s lemma to (
4.36
) yields

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{k}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma_{0,n}\|^{2}\,{\rm exp}\left(C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau-\varpi t\right)

(4.37)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}{\rm exp}\left(C\int_{\tau}^{t}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}s-\varpi(t-\tau)\right)\,{\rm d}\tau,\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

In light of the following estimate

(4.38)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\leq C,

which is a consequence of (
4.6
), (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we find that

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{k}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma_{0,n}\|^{2}\,{\rm exp}\left(C-\varpi t\right)+C\int_{0}^{t}{\rm exp}\left(C-\varpi(t-\tau)\right)\,{\rm d}\tau

(4.39)

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma_{0,n}\|^{2}\,{\rm exp}\left(C-\varpi t\right)+\frac{C}{\varpi}{\rm exp}\left(C\right),\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Next, integrating (
4.35
) in time on
(t,t+1)
for any
t\geq 0
, we get

\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\leq 4\max_{\tau\in[t,t+1]}\|\sigma^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\left(1+C\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\right)+C.

Hence, by (
4.38
) and (
4.39
), we have

(4.40)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\nabla\sigma^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\leq C\left(\|\sigma_{0,n}\|^{2}{\rm exp}(C)+\frac{C}{\varpi}{\rm exp}(C)\right)+C.

The estimates (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) entail that

\sigma^{k}\ \ \text{is uniformly bounded in}\ \ L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L_{\operatorname{uloc}}^{2}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)).

By interpolation, we easily find that
\sigma^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{4}_{\operatorname{uloc}}([0,\infty);L^{4}(\Omega))
. Finally, recalling (
4.20
) and exploiting (
4.34
) to
\sigma^{k}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0,\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(4.41)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|+|\theta_{0}|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|.

Thus, from (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and (
4.40
), we infer that

(4.42)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{4}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C.

Step 6. Estimates on time derivatives.
In what follows, we derive estimates for the time derivative of
\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k})
,
\varphi^{k}
and
\sigma^{k}
.

The estimate for
\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}
is straightforward.
By the Sobolev embedding
H^{2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\Omega)
and the estimates (
4.10
), (
4.27
), we see that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\varphi^{k}(\tau)\bm{v}^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}^{2}\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\varphi^{k}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C.

This combined with (
3.15
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and (H3) yields

(4.43)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C.

Next, we estimate
\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k})
.
From (
3.14
), for any
\bm{\zeta}\in D(\bm{S})
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}),\bm{\zeta}\rangle_{D(\bm{S})^{\prime},D(\bm{S})}=(\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}),\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta})

\displaystyle\quad=\big(\bm{v}^{k}\otimes(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}+\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{k}),\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)-\big(2\nu(\varphi^{k})D\bm{v}^{k},D\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\gamma\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{k},\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big((\mu^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}^{k}\bm{v}^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)

(4.44)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big).

The right-hand side of (
4.44
) can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle\big|\big(\bm{v}^{k}\otimes(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}+\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{k}),\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})m(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\big|\big(2\nu(\varphi^{k})D\bm{v}^{k},D\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\nu(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\gamma\big|\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{k},\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\gamma^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\gamma\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}\big)^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\nabla\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\gamma^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\gamma\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}\big)^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\big|\big((\mu^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\mu^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\mu^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+C\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}^{k}\bm{v}^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|m(\varphi^{k})\widehat{\rho}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

(4.45)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}\|\|\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\|\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|m(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|m(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\rho^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|m(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\rho^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|m(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\rho^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}

(4.46)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Collecting the above estimates, we can deduce from (
4.11
), (
4.26
) and (
4.42
) that

(4.47)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k})\|_{L^{\frac{8}{7}}(t,t+1;(D(\bm{S}))^{\prime})}\leq C,

where
C>0
depends on
\gamma
,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\|\sigma_{0,n}\|
,
\Omega
,
T
, but it is independent of
k
,
\epsilon
. In particular, the
p
-Laplacian regularization has been used to handle (
4.46
).

Finally, we estimate
\partial_{t}\sigma^{k}
. Multiplying (
3.18
) by
\zeta\in H^{1}(\Omega)
and integrating over
\Omega
, we have

\begin{split}\langle\partial_{t}\sigma^{k},\zeta\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}&=\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\zeta\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot\nabla\zeta\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k}\cdot\nabla\zeta\,{\rm d}x\\
&\leq\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\zeta\|+\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|\|\nabla\zeta\|\\
&\quad+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\zeta\|,\end{split}

which combined with the Ladyzhenskaya inequality and
(
4.10
), (
4.39
) yields

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\sigma^{k}\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|+C\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\nabla\sigma^{k}\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\sigma^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sigma^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}\|+\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\big).

Owing to (
4.11
), (
4.27
) and (
4.40
), we can conclude

(4.48)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\partial_{t}\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C,

where
C>0
depends on
\|\sigma_{0,n}\|
,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
,
T
, but is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
.

4.2.
Passage to the limit

We are ready to pass to the limit as
k\to\infty
in the semi-Galerkin scheme (
3.14
)–(
3.20
) and establish the existence of a global weak solution
(\bm{v}^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\varphi^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\mu^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\sigma^{\gamma,\epsilon,n})
to the regularized problem
(\bm{S}_{\gamma,\epsilon,n})
. For simplicity of notation, we denote the solution
(\bm{v}^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\varphi^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\mu^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\sigma^{\gamma,\epsilon,n})
by

(\bm{v}^{\sharp},\varphi^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp},\sigma^{\sharp})
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let the parameters
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
be fixed as in (
3.9
). Suppose that (H1)–(H4) are satisfied and the initial data
(\bm{v}_{0},\varphi_{0},\sigma_{0})
are given as in Theorem
2.1
. The regularized problem (
3.10
)–(
3.12
) admits a global weak solution

(\bm{v}^{\sharp},\varphi^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp},\sigma^{\sharp})

in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\sharp}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\gamma^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\bm{v}^{\sharp}\in L^{4}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)),\qquad\bm{P}\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{\sharp})\bm{v}^{\sharp})\in L^{\frac{8}{7}}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);(D(\bm{S}))^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\varphi^{\sharp}\in BC_{\mathrm{w}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{4}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{2}_{N}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mu^{\sharp}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)),\qquad\ \,\nabla\mu^{\sharp}\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{\sharp})\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{1}(\Omega)),\qquad\Psi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma^{\sharp}\in BC([0,\infty);L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\sharp})^{\frac{1}{2}}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma^{\sharp}(x,t)\geq 0\quad\text{a.e. in}\ \Omega\times(0,\infty).

and the following identities hold

\displaystyle\big\langle\partial_{t}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{\sharp})\bm{v}^{\sharp}),\bm{\zeta}\big\rangle_{(D(\bm{S}))^{\prime},D(\bm{S})}-(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{\sharp})\bm{v}^{\sharp}\otimes\bm{v}^{\sharp},D\bm{\zeta})-\big(\bm{v}^{\sharp}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\sharp},\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(2\nu(\varphi^{\sharp})D\bm{v}^{\sharp},D\bm{\zeta}\big)+\gamma\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{\sharp}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{\sharp},\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)

(4.49a)

\displaystyle\quad=\big((\mu^{\sharp}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\sigma^{\sharp})\nabla\varphi^{\sharp},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}^{\sharp}\bm{v}^{\sharp},\bm{\zeta}\big),\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{a.e. in }(0,\infty),

\displaystyle\quad\text{with}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\sharp}=-\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})m(\varphi^{\sharp})\nabla\mu^{\sharp},\quad\widehat{R}^{\sharp}=-m(\varphi^{\sharp})\nabla\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\cdot\nabla\mu^{\sharp},

(4.49b)

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\varphi^{\sharp},\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}-\big(\varphi^{\sharp}\bm{v}^{\sharp},\nabla\xi\big)+\big(m(\varphi^{\sharp})\nabla\mu^{\sharp},\nabla\xi\big)=0,\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{a.e. in }(0,\infty),

(4.49c)

\displaystyle\mu^{\sharp}=-\Delta\varphi^{\sharp}+\Psi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\sigma^{\sharp},\quad

\displaystyle\textrm{a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,\infty),

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\sigma^{\sharp},\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}-(\sigma^{\sharp}\bm{v}^{\sharp},\nabla\xi)+(\nabla\sigma^{\sharp},\nabla\xi)

(4.49d)

\displaystyle\quad=-(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\sigma^{\sharp}\nabla\varphi^{\sharp},\nabla\xi),

\displaystyle\textrm{a.e. in }(0,\infty)

for all test functions
\bm{\zeta}\in D(\bm{S})
,
\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega)
. The initial conditions are fulfilled

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\sharp}|_{t=0}=\bm{v}_{0},\quad\varphi^{\sharp}|_{t=0}=\varphi_{0,n},\quad\sigma^{\sharp}|_{t=0}=\sigma_{0,n},\quad\textrm{a.e. in }\Omega.

Moreover, the following energy inequality holds

(4.50)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\sharp}(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau\leq\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}(0),

for almost all
t>0
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{\sharp})|\bm{v}^{\sharp}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi^{\sharp}|^{2}+\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{\sharp})+\sigma^{\sharp}(\ln\sigma^{\sharp}-1)+\beta(\varphi^{\sharp})\sigma^{\sharp}\right)(t)\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\sharp}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(2\nu(\varphi^{\sharp})|D\bm{v}^{\sharp}|^{2}+\gamma|\nabla\bm{v}^{\sharp}|^{4}+m(\varphi^{\sharp})|\nabla\mu^{\sharp}|^{2}+\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{\sharp})\big|^{2}\right)(t)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

We have shown that for every integer
k\geq\widehat{k}
, the semi-Galerkin scheme (
3.14
)–(
3.20
) admits a unique global solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k},\sigma^{k})
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
with estimates that are independent of the approximation parameter
k
. In particular, the uniform estimates (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.17
),
(
4.28
), (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
), (
4.43
), (
4.47
), (
4.48
) are sufficient for us to apply theorems of weak compactness and the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma (see
[
68
]
) to extract a suitable subsequence that approaches a limit

(\bm{v}^{\sharp},\varphi^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp},\sigma^{\sharp})

in corresponding topologies as
k\to\infty
in
[0,T]
for arbitrary fixed
T>0
. With sufficient information on
\sigma^{k}
(based on the assumption that
\sigma_{0,n}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
), the convergence in (
3.18
) is standard, while the convergence for the Navier–Stokes/Cahn–Hilliard part (
3.14
)–(
3.17
) follows a similar argument for
[
26
, Lemma 3]
(see a corrigendum in
[
27
, Remark 4.3]
).
In particular, the contribution of the last term on the right-hand side of (
3.14
) converges to zero, and the convergence of
\gamma|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{k}
is a consequence of the well-known Minty’s trick for monotone operators.
Due to the lack of uniqueness for
(\bm{v}^{\sharp},\varphi^{\sharp},\mu^{\sharp},\sigma^{\sharp})
, in order to construct a global weak solution on the whole interval
[0,\infty)
, we shall combine the standard compactness argument with a diagonal extraction process as in
[
15
, Chapter V, Section 1.3.6]
for the three-dimensional Navier–Stokes system.

Integrating (
4.6
) over
[0,t]
for any
t>0
gives the energy equality

(4.51)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{k}(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau=\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(0),\quad\forall\,t>0.

Using the weak/strong convergence results, weak lower-semicontinuity of norms, and Fatou’s lemma, we can derive the energy inequality (
4.50
) from (
4.51
) following an argument similar to that in
[
26
, Section 4.1]
. Here, we present only the convergence of
\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x
. The convergence results mentioned in the following should be understood in the sense of a subsequence.
In view of (
4.18
), we write

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{k}\big|\nabla(\ln\sigma^{k}+\beta(\varphi^{k}))\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\left|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{k})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}4\big|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot\nabla\beta(\varphi^{k})\,{\rm d}x.

Since
\sigma^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
and
\partial_{t}\sigma^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})
for any
T>0
, we infer from the Aubin–Lions lemma that

\displaystyle\sigma^{k}\rightarrow\sigma^{\sharp}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\text{ and a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,T).

The pointwise convergence of
\sigma^{k}
combined with (
4.25
) yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}\quad\text{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega)),

which enables us to conclude (cf.
[
75
, Proof of Lemma 4.1]
)

(4.52)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t.

Since
\varphi^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{4}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))
and
\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})
, we infer from the Aubin–Lions lemma that

\varphi^{k}\to\varphi^{\sharp}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{4}(0,T;W^{1,4}(\Omega))\text{ and a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,T),

and thus

|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\to|\nabla\varphi^{\sharp}|^{2}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)).

Since
\beta^{\prime}
,
\beta^{\prime\prime}
are bounded, we can further deduce that

\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\rightarrow\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega)),

as well as

|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\to|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})|^{2}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega)).

These observations combined with the strong convergence of
\sigma^{k}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
and the boundedness of
\sigma^{\sharp}
in
L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega))
yield

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}\sigma^{k}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})|^{2}\sigma^{\sharp}|\nabla\varphi^{\sharp}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}(\sigma^{k}-\sigma^{\sharp})|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}(|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})|^{2}-|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})|^{2})\sigma^{\sharp}|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|

(4.53)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})|^{2}\sigma^{\sharp}(|\nabla\varphi^{k}|^{2}-|\nabla\varphi^{\sharp}|^{2})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|\to 0.

On the other hand, from the weak convergence of
\sigma^{k}
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
we infer that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot\nabla\beta(\varphi^{k})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\sigma^{\sharp}\cdot\nabla\beta(\varphi^{\sharp})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}2(\nabla\sigma^{k}-\nabla\sigma^{\sharp})\cdot\nabla\varphi^{\sharp}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp}))\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|

(4.54)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}2\nabla\sigma^{k}\cdot(\nabla\varphi^{k}-\nabla\varphi^{\sharp})\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{\sharp})\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right|\to 0.

From (
4.52
)–(
4.54
), we can conclude that

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\left|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{\sharp}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{\sharp})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\left|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{k}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{k})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x{\rm d}t,

for any
T>0
. The remainder of the limiting procedure is standard, and we omit the details.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
is complete.
∎

4.3.
Completion of the Proof of Theorem
2.1

We shall pass to the limit as
\gamma,\epsilon\to 0
and
n\to\infty
simultaneously. For this purpose, we take
\gamma=\epsilon=\frac{1}{n}
(for sufficiently large integers
n
) and simply denote the approximate solutions
(\bm{v}^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\varphi^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\mu^{\gamma,\epsilon,n},\sigma^{\gamma,\epsilon,n})
obtained in Proposition
4.1
by
(\bm{v}^{n},\varphi^{n},\mu^{n},\sigma^{n})
.

Part 1.
Let us first consider the case with
\sigma_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
.

For
\sigma_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
, we can find a family of approximations
\{\sigma_{0,n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
, with the following properties

\sigma_{0,n}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega),\quad\sigma_{0,n}\geq 0\ \text{ in }\Omega,\quad\sigma_{0,n}\not\equiv 0,\quad\sigma_{0,n}\rightarrow\sigma_{0}\ \text{ in }\ L^{2}(\Omega)\ \text{ as }n\to\infty.

It easily follows that (for sufficiently large
n
)

(4.55)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{e}^{-1}|\Omega|\leq\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{0,n}\ln\sigma_{0,n}\,{\rm d}x\leq\|\sigma_{0,n}\|^{2}\leq\|\sigma_{0}\|^{2}+1.

Hence, from the energy inequality (
4.50
) and using arguments similar to those in Section
4.1
, we can maintain uniform estimates (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.17
), (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
), (
4.43
), (
4.47
), (
4.48
) for
(\bm{v}^{n},\varphi^{n},\mu^{n},\sigma^{n})
, except (
4.28
) (depending on
\epsilon
). To recover (
4.47
), we note that at this stage, since we have already taken the limit as
k\to\infty
, it is unnecessary to handle a highly nonlinear term like in (
4.46
), that is,

\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{\zeta}\big),

for which the regularization involving the
p
-Laplacian term was essentially used (depending on
\gamma
). On the other hand, the uniform estimate for
\|\Psi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(\varphi^{n})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}
follows a direct comparison in (
4.49c
). The remaining part of the limiting procedure as
n\to\infty
can be performed analogously as in
[
26
, Sections 4.2, 4.3]
(with a corrigendum in
[
27
, Remark 4.3]
) for the Navier–Stokes part, in
[
23
,
57
]
for the Cahn–Hilliard part, and in
[
55
]
for the
\sigma^{n}
-equation. Denote the limit functions by
(\bm{v},\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
. In particular, we have

\Psi_{1/n}(\varphi^{n})\to\Psi(\varphi),\ \ \Psi^{\prime}_{1/n}(\varphi^{n})\to\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi)\quad\text{a.e. in}\ \Omega\times(0,T),

and

\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,T)),\qquad|\varphi(x,t)|<1\quad\text{for a.a.}\ (x,t)\in\Omega\times(0,T),

for any
T>0
, where the latter is a consequence of the singular nature of
\Psi
at
\pm 1
. This physical bound for
\varphi
combined the definition of
\widehat{\rho}
yields that

\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)=\rho(\varphi).

As a result, the artificial term
\widehat{R}
in the modified momentum balance equation (cf. (
3.10a
), (
3.10b
)) vanishes after passing to the limit as
n\to\infty
, that is,
\widehat{R}=0
. In addition, from the estimate

(4.56)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{n}\|_{L^{4}(0,T;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where
C>0
is independent of
n
, we see that for all
\bm{\zeta}\in L^{4}(0,T;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega))
, it holds

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\big(|\nabla\bm{v}^{n}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{n},\bm{\zeta}\big)\,{\rm d}t\right|\leq C\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\bm{\zeta}\|_{L^{4}(0,T;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega))}\to 0,\quad\text{as}\ n\to\infty.

Then, a direct comparison in (
2.24
) further yields
\partial_{t}\bm{P}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{v})\in L_{\operatorname{uloc}}^{s}([0,\infty);(D(\bm{S}))^{\prime})
, for any
s\in[1,2)
. Next, using the facts
\mu\in L^{2}_{\operatorname{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))
and
\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\in L^{2}_{\operatorname{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega))
, we can apply the classical result for the Cahn–Hilliard equation with a singular potential to conclude (see, e.g.,
[
39
]
)

(4.57)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}+\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\forall\,q\in[2,\infty).

The construction of a global weak solution on the whole interval
[0,\infty)
follows a diagonal extraction process as in
[
15
]
due to the lack of uniqueness.
Finally, using the weak/strong convergence results, weak lower-semicontinuity of norms and Fatou’s lemma, we can obtain the energy inequality (
2.20
) from (
4.50
), cf. the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

The proof of Theorem
2.1
-(2) is complete.

Part 2.
Now we study the more involved case with only
\sigma_{0}\ln\sigma_{0}\in L^{1}(\Omega)
.

The uniform estimates obtained in Step 5 of Section
4.1
are no longer valid for
(\varphi^{n},\sigma^{n})
. Thus, we lose the estimates corresponding to (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
), and the estimates of time derivatives as in (
4.47
), (
4.48
).

On the other hand, an examination of the argument in Step 4 of Section
4.1
allows us to maintain some weaker estimates. For instance, we still have

(4.58)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\sigma^{n}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\|(\sigma^{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\varphi^{n}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

which are uniform with respect to
n
. Then, a comparison in (
4.49a
) yields (cf. the treatment for (
4.44
))

(4.59)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\partial_{t}\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})\|_{L^{1}(t,t+1;(D(\bm{S}))^{\prime})}\leq C.

Here, the main modification is due to (
4.45
) (keeping in mind that the term as in (
4.46
) simply vanishes after taking
k\to\infty
), because we can only use (
4.27
) for
\varphi^{n}
instead of (
4.42
). On the other hand, since

\displaystyle\|\nabla(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})\|\leq\|\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}^{n}\|+\|\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)},

we can conclude that
\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n}
and thus
\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})
are uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(t,t+1;\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega))
for all
t\geq 0
. This fact, combined with (
4.59
) and the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma, is sufficient for the strong compactness of
\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})
and then
\bm{v}^{n}
(cf.
[
27
, Section 4.1]
). For the convenience of the readers, we sketch the proof here. First, applying the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma, we get

\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})\to\bm{w}\quad\text{strongly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)),

for any fixed
T>0
, where
\bm{w}\in L^{2}(0,T;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))
. Next, from the easy facts

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{n}\rightharpoonup\bm{v}\quad\text{weakly in}\ \ L^{4}(0,T;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\varphi^{n}\to\varphi\quad\text{strongly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\ \ \text{and a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,T),

\displaystyle\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\to\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)\quad\text{strongly in}\ \ L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega)),

we can verify that

\displaystyle\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})\rightharpoonup\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)\bm{v})\quad\text{weakly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})}\bm{v}^{n}\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)}\bm{v}\quad\text{weakly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)).

Hence,
\bm{w}=\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)\bm{v})
holds due to the uniqueness of the limit. Then using the same argument as in
[
2
, Section 5.1]
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})|\bm{v}^{n}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})\cdot\bm{v}^{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)\bm{v})\cdot\bm{v}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)|\bm{v}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t,

which combined with the uniform convexity of
L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))

yields

\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})}\bm{v}^{n}\to\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)}\bm{v}\quad\text{strongly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)).

Combining the convergence results above and the uniform positivity property of
\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})
(see (
3.3
)), we can further conclude

\bm{v}^{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})}}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})}\bm{v}^{n}\Big)\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)}}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{\rho}(\varphi)}\bm{v}\Big)=\bm{v}\quad\text{strongly in}\ \ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)).

We also note that (
4.59
) is not enough to guarantee a weak limit for
\partial_{t}\bm{P}(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{n})\bm{v}^{n})
. This motivates a weaker formulation of the momentum balance equation for the fluid velocity field
\bm{v}
(see (
2.14
)).

In the following, we say more words about the convergence involving
\sigma^{n}
. A comparison in (
4.49d
) yields

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\sigma^{n}\|_{(H^{2}_{N}(\Omega))^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{v}^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{n}\|+C\|\sigma^{n}\|+C\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{n})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{n}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+\|\bm{v}^{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{v}^{n}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\nabla\varphi^{n}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\varphi^{n}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\|\sigma^{n}\|

which implies

(4.60)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\partial_{t}\sigma^{n}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(t,t+1;(H^{2}_{N}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C,

where
C>0
is independent of
n
. To show the strong convergence of
\sigma^{n}
, we apply a compactness theorem due to Dubinskii (see, e.g.,
[
24
,
11
]
):

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
T>0
. Suppose that
B_{0}
is a semi-normed set
that is compactly embedded into a Banach space
B
, which is continuously embedded into
another Banach space
B_{1}
. Then, for any
1\leq p,q<\infty
, the following embedding is compact:

\big\{f\ \big|\ f\in L^{p}(0,T;B_{0}),\ \partial_{t}f\in L^{q}(0,T;B_{1})\big\}\ \hookrightarrow\ L^{p}(0,T;B).

Define

B=L^{1}(\Omega),\quad B_{0}=\big\{f\in B\ \big|\ f\geq 0\ \ \text{a.e. in}\ \Omega,\ f^{\frac{1}{2}}\in H^{1}(\Omega)\big\},\quad B_{1}=(H_{N}^{2}(\Omega))^{\prime},

where
B_{0}
is a semi-normed space with the semi-norm

[f]_{B_{0}}=\|f\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla f^{\frac{1}{2}}\|^{2}.

The continuity of the embedding
B\hookrightarrow B_{1}
is obvious, and the compactness of the embedding
B_{0}\hookrightarrow B
has been verified in
[
12
]
. It follows from (
4.58
) and (
4.60
) that

\sigma^{n}\ \text{is uniformly bounded in}\ \ \big\{f\ \big|\ f\in L^{1}(0,T;B_{0}),\ \partial_{t}f\in L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,T;B_{1})\big\}.

Thus, we can apply Lemma
4.1
to conclude

\sigma^{n}\to\sigma\quad\text{strongly in}\ L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))\cong L^{1}(\Omega\times(0,T)),

as
n\to\infty
(up to a subsequence). This yields

\sigma^{n}\to\sigma
almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,T)
. Using (
4.58
) and interpolation, we also have

(4.61)

\displaystyle\sigma^{n}\to\sigma\quad\text{strongly in}\ L^{2-r}(\Omega\times(0,T)),\quad\forall\,r\in(0,1).

In addition, from the facts
\sigma\ln\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{1}(\Omega))
,
\partial_{t}\sigma\in L^{\frac{4}{3}}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);(H^{2}_{N}(\Omega))^{\prime})
, we can apply the argument in
[
12
, Section 12]
to obtain
\sigma\in BC_{\mathrm{w}}([0,\infty);L^{1}(\Omega))
.

Keeping these modifications in mind, we can take the limit as
n\to\infty
to establish the existence of a global finite energy solution
(\bm{v},\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
on
[0,\infty)
again through a diagonal extraction process as in
[
15
]
. Finally, using the weak/strong convergence results, weak lower-semicontinuity of norms, and Fatou’s lemma, we can recover the energy inequality (
2.20
) from (
4.50
).
However, a different treatment for the dissipation term
\int_{0}^{t}\!\int_{\Omega}\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}\tau
is required here, compared to the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
For any
T>0
, we infer from (
4.50
) that
\int_{0}^{T}\!\int_{\Omega}\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t
is uniformly bounded with respect to
n
. This implies

2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\rightharpoonup\bm{g}\quad\text{weakly in}\ L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega)),

for some
\bm{g}\in L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))
, and as a consequence,

(4.62)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\bm{g}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t.

Next, we observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}-\sqrt{\sigma}\big|^{3}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\sigma^{n}-\sigma|\big|\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}-\sqrt{\sigma}\big|\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\sigma^{n}-\sigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}-\sqrt{\sigma}\big|^{3}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}.

This together with (
4.61
) (taking
r=1/2
) yields

(4.63)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\to\sqrt{\sigma}\quad\text{strongly in }L^{3}(0,T;L^{3}(\Omega)).

In addition, from the strong convergence
\varphi^{n}\to\varphi
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\Omega))
, (H4) on
\beta
and the boundedness of
\nabla\varphi^{n}
in
L^{4}(0,T;\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega))
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})-\nabla\beta(\varphi)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{n})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)|^{2}|\nabla\varphi^{n}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t+2\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)|^{2}|\nabla\varphi^{n}-\nabla\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\to 0.

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\to\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla\beta(\varphi)\quad\text{strongly in }L^{\frac{6}{5}}(0,T;L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\Omega)).

On the other hand, it follows from (
4.58
) and (
4.63
) that

\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\sigma}\quad\text{weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega)).

As a consequence, we can identify
\bm{g}=2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma}+\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla\beta(\varphi)
,
which combined with (
4.62
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma}+\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla\beta(\varphi)\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\int_{\Omega}\big|2\nabla\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}+\sqrt{\sigma^{n}}\nabla\beta(\varphi^{n})\big|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}t.

The proof of Theorem
2.1
-(1) is complete.

\square

4.4.
Proof of Corollary
2.1

We recall that the approximate solution
\sigma^{k}
considered in Section
4.1
satisfies

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\sigma^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{t}^{t+1}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(\tau)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}\tau\leq C,\quad\forall\,k\geq\widehat{k}.

Thus, an application of the uniform Gronwall lemma (see
[
70
, Chapter III, Lemma 1.1]
) to (
4.33
) implies that

\|\sigma^{k}(t+\tau)\|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\tau},\quad\forall\,t\geq 0,\quad\forall\,\tau\in(0,1],

where
C>0
depends on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
and coefficients of the system, but it is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
(and also
\|\sigma_{0,n}\|
),
t
and
\tau
. Based on this fact, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq\tau}\|\sigma^{k}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C(\tau),\quad\sup_{t\geq\tau}\|\varphi^{k}\|_{L^{4}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C(\tau),

where the positive constant
C(\tau)
depends on
\tau
,
\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
\Omega
and the coefficients of the system, but it is independent of
k
,
\gamma
,
\epsilon
,
n
(and also
