\|\sigma_{0,n}\|
),
t
. Moreover,
C(\tau)
explodes as
\tau\to 0^{+}
.
Taking
k\to\infty
, the same properties hold for the approximate solutions obtained in Proposition
4.1
.
Since
\tau\in(0,1]
is arbitrary, repeating the procedure in Section
4.3
, we can obtain a global finite energy solution defined on
[0,\infty)
that becomes a global weak solution for
t>0
.

The proof of Corollary
2.1
is complete.

\square

5.
Regularity Results for Auxiliary Decoupled Systems

In this section, we investigate some auxiliary problems related to the coupled system (
2.8
)–(
2.9
). They will play a crucial role in the
sequel in establishing the global strong well-posedness of problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) and the propagation of regularity for global weak solutions.

5.1.
Diffusion equation with divergence-free drift

Let
\bm{v}
,
\varphi
be two given functions with suitable regularity properties. Consider the following diffusion equation with convection:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\sigma+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\sigma-\Delta\sigma=\mathrm{div}\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\nabla\varphi\big),\quad\text{in}\ \Omega\times(0,\infty),

equipped with the following boundary and initial conditions

(5.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{\bm{n}}\sigma=0\quad\text{on}\ \partial\Omega\times(0,\infty),\qquad\sigma|_{t=0}=\sigma_{0}\quad\text{in}\ \Omega.

First, we prove the existence and uniqueness of a global weak solution to problem
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
).

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with a
C^{2}
boundary.
Assume that (H4) is satisfied and

\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),\quad\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{2}_{N}(\Omega)).

Then, for any initial datum
\sigma_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
satisfying
\sigma_{0}\geq 0
almost everywhere in
\Omega
, problem (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) admits a unique global weak solution
\sigma
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
such that

(5.3)

\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}),

for any
T>0
, with
\sigma(x,t)\geq 0
almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
.
Moreover, it holds

(5.4)

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\sigma,\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}-(\sigma\bm{v},\nabla\xi)+(\nabla\sigma,\nabla\xi)+\left(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\nabla\varphi,\nabla\xi\right)=0,\quad\forall\,\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega),

almost everywhere in
(0,\infty)
, and
\sigma|_{t=0}=\sigma_{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
.

Proof.

The existence of a nonnegative global weak solution follows from the construction of smooth approximate solutions (see
[
40
, Lemma 3.1]
), the
a priori
estimates (see below) combined with the compactness method.
Testing (
5.4
) with
1
, we get

(5.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\sigma(t)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\sigma_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x,\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Next, a modification of the argument in Section 4.1 (see Step 5) yields that

(5.6)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\sigma\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}\leq C\left(1+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)\|\sigma\|^{2}.

By Gronwall’s lemma, we have

(5.7)

\displaystyle\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq K_{2},\quad\|\sigma\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq K_{2},

for any
T>0
, where
K_{2}>0
depends on
\|\sigma_{0}\|
,
\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}
,
T
. Moreover, similar to (
4.48
), from the assumptions on
\bm{v}
and
\varphi
, by a comparison in (
5.4
), we can deduce that
\partial_{t}\sigma\in L^{2}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})
.

Now we prove the uniqueness. Let
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
be two weak solutions to problem (
5.1
)–(
5.2
), corresponding to the initial data
\sigma_{0,1}
and
\sigma_{0,2}
, respectively. Define the difference

\widetilde{\sigma}=\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2}
, which solves

(5.8)

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\sigma},\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}-(\bm{v}\widetilde{\sigma},\nabla\xi)+(\nabla\widetilde{\sigma},\nabla\xi)+(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\widetilde{\sigma}\nabla\varphi,\nabla\xi)=0,\quad\forall\,\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega),

almost everywhere in
(0,T)
for any fixed
T>0
. Taking
\xi=\widetilde{\sigma}
in (
5.8
), after integration by parts, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\widetilde{\sigma}\nabla\varphi,\nabla\widetilde{\sigma})

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\widetilde{\sigma}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\widetilde{\sigma}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{2}+C\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\widetilde{\sigma}\|^{2}.

An application of Gronwall’s lemma yields the continuous dependence estimate

\|\sigma_{1}(t)-\sigma_{2}(t)\|^{2}\leq\|\sigma_{0,1}-\sigma_{0,2}\|^{2}\mathrm{exp}\left(C\int_{0}^{t}\|\varphi(s)\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right),\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

which guarantees the uniqueness of the weak solution.

The proof of Proposition
5.1
is complete.
∎

The following proposition yields the strong well-posedness of problem
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
).

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with a
C^{2}
boundary. Assume that (H4) is satisfied and

\displaystyle\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);W^{2,q}(\Omega)\cap H^{2}_{N}(\Omega)),

for some
q>2
. Then for any initial datum
\sigma_{0}\in H^{1}(\Omega)
satisfying
\sigma_{0}\geq 0
almost everywhere in
\Omega
, problem
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
) admits a unique global strong solution
\sigma
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
such that

(5.10)

\begin{split}\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),\end{split}

for any
T>0
, with
\sigma(x,t)\geq 0
almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
,
which satisfies (
5.1
) almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
. Moreover, we have
\partial_{\bm{n}}\sigma=0
almost everywhere on
\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty)
and
\sigma|_{t=0}=\sigma_{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
.

Proof.

We only derive the necessary higher-order
a priori
estimates.
Multiplying (
5.1
) by
-\Delta\sigma
and integrating over
\Omega
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+\left\|\Delta\sigma\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\sigma\Delta\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\nabla\varphi\big)\Delta\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x.

Using Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality, the Sobolev embedding theorem, the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality, the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality and (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\sigma\Delta\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\Delta\sigma\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}\|\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma\|(\|\Delta\sigma\|+\overline{\sigma_{0}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\Delta\sigma\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+C,

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\nabla\varphi\big)\Delta\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma\|\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q-2}}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}_{\bm{L}^{2q}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|

\displaystyle+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q-2}}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\varphi\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\sigma\|\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|+C\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla\varphi\|+1)\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}\|\Delta\sigma\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\Delta\sigma\|^{2}+C\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}^{2}(\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+1).

From the above estimates, we can derive the following differential inequality

(5.11)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+\|\Delta\sigma\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}^{2}\big)\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+C\big(\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}^{2}+1\big).

Applying Gronwall’s lemma and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, we obtain

(5.12)

\displaystyle\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq K_{3},\quad\|\sigma\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq K_{3},

for any
T>0
, where the constant
K_{3}>0
depends on
\|\sigma_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}
,

\|\bm{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))}
,
\|\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))}
,

\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,
\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}
, and
T
. Finally, by a comparison in the equation (
5.1
), it is straightforward to check that
\partial_{t}\sigma\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
.

The proof of Proposition
5.2
is complete.
∎

Finally, we derive an
L^{\infty}
-estimate of
\sigma
under some additional assumptions on
\sigma_{0}
and
\varphi
.

Corollary 5.1
.

Let the assumptions in Proposition
5.2
be satisfied. Assume in addition,

\sigma_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega))\quad\text{for some }q>2.

Then, we have

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq C,

where
C>0
depends on
\|\sigma_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}
,
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

For any
p\geq 2
, multiplying (
5.1
) by
\sigma^{p-1}
and integrating over
\Omega
, exploiting the incompressibility condition and the no-slip boundary condition for
\bm{v}
, we find

\frac{1}{p}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\frac{4(p-1)}{p^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\sigma^{\frac{p}{2}}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x=-(p-1)\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma^{p-1}\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla\sigma\,{\rm d}x.

Observing that

\displaystyle\left|(p-1)\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma^{p-1}\nabla\varphi\cdot\nabla\sigma\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2(p-1)}{p}\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\big\|\sigma^{\frac{p}{2}}\big\|\big\|\nabla\sigma^{\frac{p}{2}}\big\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2(p-1)}{p^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\sigma^{\frac{p}{2}}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\frac{p-1}{2}\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and using the Sobolev embedding theorem
W^{2,q}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)
for
q>2
, we get

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{p}\,{\rm d}x+\frac{2(p-1)}{p}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\sigma^{\frac{p}{2}}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq Cp(p-1)\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x,

where the constant
C>0
is independent of the exponent
p
. This allows us to apply the Moser–Alikakos iteration technique
[
10
]
. By the same argument as that for
[
72
, Lemma 3.2]
, we can deduce from differential inequality (
5.14
) that the
L^{\infty}
-estimate (
5.13
) holds. The details are left to the interested reader.
∎

5.2.
Cahn–Hilliard equation with divergence-free drift and conservative forcing

Let
\bm{v}
and
\sigma
be two given functions with suitable regularity properties. We consider the following Cahn–Hilliard equation with divergence-free drift and conservative forcing

(5.15)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi=\mathrm{div}(m(\varphi)\nabla\mu),\\
\mu=-\Delta\varphi+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi)+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma,\end{cases}\quad\text{ in }\Omega\times(0,\infty),

subject to the boundary and initial conditions

(5.16)

\begin{cases}\partial_{\bm{n}}\varphi=\partial_{\bm{n}}\mu=0\quad&\text{ on }\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty),\\
\varphi|_{t=0}=\varphi_{0}\quad&\text{ in }\Omega.\end{cases}

First, we establish the existence and uniqueness of a global weak solution to problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
). This is a generalization of
[
1
, Theorem 6]
(see also
[
5
, Remark 2.2]
) and
[
19
, Theorem 1.2 - (A), (B)]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with a
C^{2}
boundary. Assume that (H1), (H3) and (H4) are satisfied, and

\bm{v}\in L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty,L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L_{\operatorname{uloc}}^{2}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)).

Then, for any initial datum
\varphi_{0}\in H^{1}(\Omega)
with
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1
and
\left|\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right|<1
, problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
) admits a unique global weak solution
(\varphi,\mu)
in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
such that

(1)

The weak solution satisfies

\displaystyle\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{4}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;W^{2,q}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,\infty))\ \text{ such that }|\varphi(x,t)|<1\ \text{a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,\infty),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T;V_{0}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\mu\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega)),\quad\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega)),

for any
q\in[2,\infty)
and
T>0
.

(2)

The weak solution solves (
5.15
) in a variational sense as follows:

(5.17)

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\varphi,\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}+(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi,\xi)+(m(\varphi)\nabla\mu,\nabla\xi)=0,\quad\forall\,\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega),\ \text{a.e. in }(0,\infty),

with

\mu=-\Delta\varphi+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi)+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\quad\text{a.e. in}\ \ \Omega\times(0,\infty).

Besides,
\partial_{\bm{n}}\varphi=0
holds almost everywhere on
\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty)
and

\varphi|_{t=0}=\varphi_{0}
holds almost everywhere in
\Omega
.

(3)

The weak solution satisfies the following energy equality

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{free}}(\varphi(t))+\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}s

(5.18)

\displaystyle\quad=E_{\mathrm{free}}(\varphi_{0})-\int_{0}^{t}\big(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi,\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\big)\,{\rm d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\big(m(\varphi)\nabla\mu,\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\big)\,{\rm d}s,

for every
t\geq 0
, where

E_{\mathrm{free}}(\varphi)=\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi(\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

The existence of a global weak solution to problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
) can be proven by regularizing the singular potential and applying a suitable Faedo–Galerkin approximation scheme analogous to that in Section
3
(or, alternatively, by exploiting the viscous regularization as in
[
5
]
). We refer to, e.g.,
[
63
, Lemma 2.4]
for further details of the approximating procedure, where the simplified case with
\bm{v}=\mathbf{0}
,
\sigma=0
was treated. In the following, we only derive necessary formal
a priori
estimates.

Integrating (
5.15
)
1
over
\Omega
, using the incompressibility and the no-slip boundary condition of the velocity field
\bm{v}
, we get

(5.19)

\int_{\Omega}\varphi(t)\,{\rm d}x=\int_{\Omega}\varphi_{0}\,{\rm d}x,\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Multiplying (
5.15
)
1
by

\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma
, integrating over
\Omega
and exploiting the definition of
\mu
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+\Psi(\varphi)\Big)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\,{\rm d}x,

for almost every
t>0
. To proceed, we assume the
a priori
estimate

(5.21)

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq 1.

which can be guaranteed by the singularity of
\Psi
, see Remark
5.1
below for further comments.
By the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma\|+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\sigma\|+C\|\nabla\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Delta\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla\sigma\|+\|\sigma\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then, exploiting (
5.21
), (
5.22
), (H4), the assumptions on
(\bm{v},\sigma)
and Young’s inequality, we see that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\mu)\varphi\,{\rm d}x\right|+\left|\int_{\Omega}\varphi\bm{v}\cdot\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\mu\|+C\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\sigma\|

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Delta\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla\sigma\|+\|\sigma\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{m_{*}}{8}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\varphi\|\|\Delta\varphi\|\|\sigma\|(\|\nabla\sigma\|+\|\sigma\|)+C\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}

(5.23)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{8}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C(\|\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2})+C(\|\sigma\|^{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+\|\sigma\|^{4})\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}.

Using (
5.22
), by a similar argument for (
5.23
), we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\,{\rm d}x\right|\leq\|m(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu\|\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\|

(5.24)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{8}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+C(\|\sigma\|^{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+\|\sigma\|^{4})\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}.

In order to estimate
\|\Delta\varphi\|
, let us consider the following elliptic problem

(5.25)

\begin{cases}-\Delta\varphi+\Psi_{0}(\varphi)=\mu+\theta_{0}\varphi-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma,&\quad\text{in}\ \Omega\times(0,T),\\
\partial_{\bm{n}}\varphi=0,&\quad\text{on}\ \partial\Omega\times(0,T).\end{cases}

Testing (
5.25
)
1
by
-\Delta\varphi
yields that

\displaystyle\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)|\nabla\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla\varphi\,{\rm d}x+\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\cdot\nabla\varphi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mu\|\|\nabla\varphi\|+|\theta_{0}|\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma\|\|\nabla\varphi\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{*}}{16}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\nabla\varphi\|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Delta\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla\sigma\|+\|\sigma\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{16}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\quad+C(1+\|\sigma\|^{4}+\|\sigma\|^{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2})\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}.

Combining (
5.23
), (
5.24
), (
5.26
) and the assumption
\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty,L^{2}(\Omega))
, we deduce from (
5.20
) and (H1) that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+\Psi(\varphi)\Big)\,{\rm d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(5.27)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(1+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2})\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}+\Psi(\varphi)\Big)\,{\rm d}x+C(1+\|\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}).

An application of Gronwall’s lemma yields

(5.28)

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi(t)\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi(\varphi(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\right)+\frac{m_{*}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\mu(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}s\leq C,\end{split}

for any
T>0
, where the positive constant
C
depends on
E_{\text{free}}(\varphi_{0})
,
\|\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma(\Omega)})}
,

\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\theta
,
\theta_{0}
,
\Omega
and
T
. Combining (
5.19
) and (
5.28
), we obtain

(5.29)

\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C_{0},\quad\|\nabla\mu\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C_{0},\quad\forall\,T>0,

where the positive constant
C_{0}
depends on
E_{\text{free}}(\varphi_{0})
,
|\overline{\varphi_{0}}|
,
\theta
,
\theta_{0}
,
\Omega
,
\|\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma(\Omega)})}
,

\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))}
, and
T
.

Recall the well-known inequality (see, for instance,
[
58
]
)

(5.30)

\int_{\Omega}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\right|\,{\rm d}x\leq C_{1}\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\left(\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right)\,{\rm d}x+C_{2},

where
C_{1}>0
depends only on
\overline{\varphi_{0}}\in(-1,1)
and
C_{2}>0
depends only on
\theta
and
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
. Multiplying (
5.15
)
2
by
\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}}
(cf. (
5.19
)), we find

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\left(\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Omega}(\mu-\overline{\mu})\left(\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right)\,{\rm d}x+\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}\varphi\left(\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right)\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma(\varphi-\overline{\varphi_{0}})\,{\rm d}x.

Then by the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, (
5.30
) and (H3), we reach

(5.31)

\int_{\Omega}\left|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\right|\,{\rm d}x\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|\left(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\varphi\|+\|\sigma\|\right)+C,

where
C
depends only on
\theta_{0}
,
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
\Omega
. Since
\overline{\mu}=\overline{\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)}-\theta_{0}\overline{\varphi}+\overline{\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma}
, we infer from (
5.19
) and (
5.31
) that

|\overline{\mu}|\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|\left(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\varphi\|+\|\sigma\|\right)+C.

Applying the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality again, we have

(5.32)

\|\mu\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|\left(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\varphi\|+\|\sigma\|\right)+C\|\nabla\mu\|+C,

which together with (
5.29
) yields that
\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
is bounded for any fixed
T>0
.

Noticing that
\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}
for any
q\geq 2
. Applying
[
20
, Lemma A.1]
to problem (
5.25
), we infer from (
5.29
), (
5.32
) and the elliptic regularity theory that

(5.33)

\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}+\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\right),

for any
q\geq 2
.
The positive constant
C
in (
5.33
) depends on
q
,
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
,
\theta
,
\theta_{0}
,
\Omega
,
C_{0}
,
C_{1},C_{2}
.
Thus, we can deduce from (
5.32
), (
5.33
) that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}\leq C_{3},\quad\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq C_{3},\quad\forall\,T>0.

Besides, exploiting the proof of (
5.26
) and using the estimate (
5.29
), we find

\displaystyle\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mu\|+C(1+\|\nabla\sigma\|)+C\|\Delta\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|\nabla\sigma\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.35)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\varphi\|^{2}+C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\nabla\sigma\|\right),

which implies

(5.36)

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C_{4}.

In addition, a comparison argument in (
5.15
)
1
yields

(5.37)

\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|m(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu\|,

so that

(5.38)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C_{5}.

Next, we prove the uniqueness of the weak solution. Let
(\varphi_{1},\mu_{1})
and
(\varphi_{2},\mu_{2})
be two weak solutions to problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
), corresponding to the initial data
\varphi_{0,1}
and
\varphi_{0,2}
satisfying
\overline{\varphi_{0,1}}=\overline{\varphi_{0,2}}
.
We define the differences
\widetilde{\varphi}=\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}
,
\widetilde{\mu}=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
, which solve

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi},\xi\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}+(m(\varphi_{1})\nabla\widetilde{\mu},\nabla\xi)

(5.39)

\displaystyle\quad=-(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{\varphi},\xi)-((m(\varphi_{1})-m(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\mu_{2},\nabla\xi),\quad\forall\,\xi\in H^{1}(\Omega),\ \text{a.e. in }(0,\infty),

where

(5.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}=-\Delta\widetilde{\varphi}+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})+(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\sigma.

Taking
\xi=\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}
in (
5.39
), we find

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi},\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+(\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}),\widetilde{\varphi})

\displaystyle\quad=\theta_{0}\|\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+(\widetilde{\varphi}\bm{v},\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi})-((m(\varphi_{1})-m(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\mu_{2},\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi})

(5.41)

\displaystyle\qquad-((\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\sigma,\widetilde{\varphi}).

The four terms on the right-hand side of (
5.41
) can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle\theta_{0}\|\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|(\widetilde{\varphi}\bm{v},\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|((m(\varphi_{1})-m(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\mu_{2},\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla\mu_{2}\|^{2}+\|\varphi_{1}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}\right)\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|((\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\sigma,\widetilde{\varphi})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\widetilde{\varphi}\|\|\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\left(1+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

where the third estimate follows from
[
19
, (3.51)]
. We now handle the term
\langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi},\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}
on the left-hand side. Recalling
[
19
, (3.16)]
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi},\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\rangle_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime},H^{1}(\Omega)}=\int_{\Omega}(\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi})\,\widetilde{\varphi}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi},\widetilde{\varphi})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mathcal{N}\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\cdot m^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\nabla\varphi_{1}\left|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\right|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(5.42)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mathcal{N}\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\cdot m^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\left(D^{2}\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\right)\,{\rm d}x,

almost everywhere in
(0,\infty)
. Here,
D^{2}f
denotes the Hessian of a given scalar function
f
. We note that

(\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi},\widetilde{\varphi})^{\frac{1}{2}}=\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\big\|

is a norm in
V^{-1}_{(0)}
, which is equivalent to

\|\nabla\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\varphi}\|
.
The second and third terms on the right-hand side of (
5.42
) have been estimated in
[
19
, (3.47), (3.50)]
such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mathcal{N}\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\cdot m^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\nabla\varphi_{1}\left|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\right|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\left(\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\|_{V^{-1}_{(0)}}^{2}+\|\varphi_{1}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}\right)\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\mathcal{N}\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\cdot m^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\left(D^{2}\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\right)\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{12}\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}+C\left(\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\|_{V^{-1}_{(0)}}^{2}+\|\varphi_{1}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}\right)\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2},

with some minor adjustments in the coefficients here due to Young’s inequality. Recalling that
\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|
and
\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\|
are equivalent norms, combining the above estimates and using the convexity of
\Psi_{0}
, we can deduce from (
5.41
) that

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\big\|^{2}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\varphi}\|^{2}\leq h_{1}(t)\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}\widetilde{\varphi}\big\|^{2},

where

h_{1}(\cdot)=C\Big(1+\|\nabla\mu_{2}\|^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{1}\|_{V^{-1}_{(0)}}^{2}+\|\varphi_{1}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}+\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\sigma\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\Big)\in L^{1}(0,T),\quad\forall\,T>0.

An application of Gronwall’s lemma entails that

\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}(\varphi_{1}(t)-\varphi_{2}(t))\big\|^{2}\leq\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi_{1})}\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi_{1}}(\varphi_{0,1}-\varphi_{0,2})\big\|^{2}\mathrm{e}^{\int_{0}^{t}h(s)\,{\rm d}s},\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Hence, if
\varphi_{0,1}=\varphi_{0,2}
, then we get the uniqueness of a weak solution.

Finally, we note that the regularity of
(\varphi,\mu)
allows us to test (
5.17
) with
\xi=\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma
. Integrating the resultant on
[0,t]
gives the energy identity (
5.18
).

The proof of Proposition
5.3
is complete.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

The
L^{\infty}
-estimate (
5.21
) is not available at the level of Galerkin approximation (along with a regularization of the singular potential
\Psi
). However, we can first prove the existence of a (unique) weak solution
(\varphi^{n},\mu^{n})
under sufficiently smooth given data
(\bm{v}^{n},\sigma^{n})
that approximate
(\bm{v},\sigma)
(cf. the estimate (
5.23
) where (
5.21
) was essentially used). The approximate solution
\varphi^{n}
satisfies (
5.21
) due to the singular nature of
\Psi
. Then the associated estimates independent of
n
can be recovered from the energy identity (
5.18
) for
\varphi^{n}
. Afterwards, we can pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
. By the compactness argument, we can obtain the existence of a global weak solution to the original problem.

The following result presents the existence and uniqueness of a global strong solution under additional assumptions on
(\bm{v},\sigma)
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with a
C^{3}
boundary. Assume that (H1), (H3) and (H4) are satisfied.
Let

\displaystyle\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))

for some
q>2
and any
T>0
, with the additional property
\sigma(\cdot,0)\in H^{1}(\Omega)
.
Then, for any initial datum
\varphi_{0}\in H^{2}_{N}(\Omega)
with

\|\varphi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1
,
\left|\overline{\varphi_{0}}\right|<1
and
\mu_{0}:=-\Delta\varphi_{0}+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
, problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
) admits a unique global strong solution in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which satisfies

(5.43)

\begin{split}&\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{2,q}(\Omega)),\\
&\partial_{t}\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}),\\
&\varphi\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,\infty))\ \text{ with }\ |\varphi(x,t)|<1\ \text{a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,\infty),\\
&\mu\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{4}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega)),\quad\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega)),\end{split}

for any
T>0
.
The strong solution satisfies (
5.15
) almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, the boundary conditions
\partial_{\bm{n}}\varphi=\partial_{\bm{n}}\mu=0
almost everywhere on

\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, and the initial condition
\varphi|_{t=0}=\varphi_{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
.
In addition, if
\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
, then we have
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega))
and there exists a constant
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}=\widetilde{\delta}_{1}(T)\in(0,1)
such that

(5.44)

\|\varphi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1-\widetilde{\delta}_{1},\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

where
\widetilde{\delta}_{1}
depends on
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}
,
\|\nabla(-\Delta\varphi_{0}+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{0}))\|
,
\|\nabla\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
, coefficients of the system,
\Omega
and
T
.

Proof.

We extend the arguments devised in
[
5
, Theorem 2.4]
(with a constant mobility and a divergence-free drift, without the conservative forcing term) and
[
19
, Section 4]
(with a variable mobility, without the divergence-free drift and the conservative forcing term).

Multiplying (
5.15
)
1
by
\partial_{t}\mu
and integrating over
\Omega
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\varphi\,\partial_{t}\mu\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\,\partial_{t}\mu\,{\rm d}x=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}m^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

By definition of
\mu
, we observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\varphi\,\partial_{t}\mu\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)|\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x-\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\partial_{t}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

Exploiting the incompressibility and the no-slip boundary condition of the velocity field, after integration by parts, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\,\partial_{t}\mu\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\left(-\Delta\partial_{t}\varphi+\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi-\theta_{0}\partial_{t}\varphi+\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\right)\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\left(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\underbrace{\int_{\partial\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)(\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\cdot{\bm{n}})\,{\rm d}S}_{=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}\cdot\left(\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\nabla\varphi\right)\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\,\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{T}\nabla\varphi\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla^{2}\varphi\,\bm{v}\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\,{\rm d}x+\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\varphi\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

Then, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi)|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)|\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{T}\nabla\varphi\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla^{2}\varphi\,\bm{v}\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\,{\rm d}x-\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\varphi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)(\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x

(5.45)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}m^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Combining (
5.32
), (
5.33
) with the assumption
\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))
for
some given
q>2
and any
T>0
, we observe that

(5.46)

\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}+\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq C_{q}\left(1+\|\nabla\mu\|\right),\quad\text{for a.a. }t\in[0,T].

Now we estimate the terms on the right-hand side of (
5.45
). Recalling that
\partial_{t}\varphi
is mean-free, thanks to the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, Young’s inequality, (
5.29
) and (
5.37
), we find

\displaystyle|\theta_{0}|\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\varphi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}.

Exploiting (
5.46
) and the Sobolev embedding theorem (with
q>2
), we infer that (cf.
[
5
]
)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla\bm{v}^{T}\nabla\varphi\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla^{2}\varphi\,\bm{v}\right)\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{W^{2,q}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{\frac{2q}{q-2}}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|\nabla\mu\|\right)\|\nabla\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{\frac{2q}{q-2}}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}.

Next, we obtain from (
5.29
), (
5.37
) and (
5.46
) that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)(\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|(\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|+\|\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\|)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q-2}}(\Omega)}\|\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}(\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|+\|\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\|)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C(1+\|\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}^{2})\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C(1+\|\sigma\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}^{2})\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\left(\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}\right)+C\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,4}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C(\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|^{2}),

while using (
5.21
), we get

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{0}\int_{\Omega}(\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi)\varphi\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{v}\|\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}\|^{2}.

The positive constants
C
in all the above estimates may depend on the parameters of the system, such as
\theta_{0}
,
\theta
,
\Omega
,
\overline{\varphi_{0}}
, and
T>0
(note that we have used (
5.29
) and
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}
).
Finally, we treat the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.45
). Observe that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}m^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|\|\nabla\mu\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}^{2}

(5.47)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mu\|\|\nabla\mu\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}.

Thus, it remains to estimate
\|\nabla\mu\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}
.
Using the idea in
[
19
]
, we rewrite (
5.15
)
1
as

(5.48)

\displaystyle\mu-\overline{\mu}=-\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}(\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi),

where
\overline{\mu}=\overline{\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)}-\theta_{0}\overline{\varphi}+\overline{\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma}
. Applying the elliptic estimate (
2.4
), we find

\displaystyle\|\mu-\overline{\mu}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\varphi\|\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{\varphi}(\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\|+\|\partial_{t}\varphi+\bm{v}\cdot\nabla\varphi\|\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu\|+C\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.49)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}.

Using (
5.29
), (
5.37
), (
5.49
), and the assumption
\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))
,
we infer from (
5.47
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}m^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\mu\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|(\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\mu\|)\|\nabla\mu\|

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|\nabla\mu\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mu\|\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

(5.50)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}+C(1+\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}+\|\nabla\mu\|^{2})\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+C\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}.

Collecting the above estimates, we can deduce from (
5.35
) and (
5.45
) that

(5.51)

\begin{split}&\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi)}\nabla\mu\big\|^{2}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}\leq h_{2}(t)\big\|\sqrt{m(\varphi)}\nabla\mu\big\|^{2}+h_{3}(t),\end{split}

for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, with
T>0
being arbitrary, where

\displaystyle h_{2}(\cdot)=C\big(1+\|\sqrt{m(\varphi)}\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}\big)\in L^{1}(0,T),

\displaystyle h_{3}(\cdot)=C\big(1+\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|^{2}\big)\in L^{1}(0,T).

An application of Gronwall’s lemma entails that

(5.52)

\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\nabla\mu(t)\|^{2}&\leq\frac{1}{m_{*}}\left(m^{*}\|\nabla\mu(0)\|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}h_{3}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\right)e^{\int_{0}^{T}h_{2}(s)\,{\rm d}s},\end{split}

where we have used (H3). Noticing that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mu(0)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla(\mu_{0}+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})\sigma(0))\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma(0)\|+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\sigma(0)\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+C(\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+1)\|\sigma(0)\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+C(1+\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+\|\nabla\sigma(0)\|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma(0)\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)},

where the constant
C>0
depends on
\|\sigma_{0}\|
and
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}
.
Therefore, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\nabla\mu(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|^{2}+C(1+\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+\|\nabla\sigma(0)\|)\|\sigma(0)\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)e^{\int_{0}^{T}h_{2}(s)\,{\rm d}s}

\displaystyle\quad+Ce^{\int_{0}^{T}h_{2}(s)\,{\rm d}s}\int_{0}^{T}h_{3}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

(5.53)

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{F}(T).

Integrating (
5.51
) on
[0,T]
, and exploiting (
5.53
), we further obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|^{2}+C(1+\|\nabla\mu_{0}\|+\|\nabla\sigma(0)\|)\|\sigma(0)\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)

(5.54)

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathcal{F}(T)\int_{0}^{T}h_{2}(s)\,{\rm d}s+\int_{0}^{T}h_{3}(s)\,{\rm d}s.

Thus, we can conclude

(5.55)

\|\nabla\mu\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

for any
T>0
. Then it follows from (
5.32
), (
5.46
) and (
5.55
) that

(5.56)

\displaystyle\|\mu\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,

(5.57)

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq C.

By a comparison in (
5.37
), we also obtain

(5.58)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C.

As a consequence, from (
5.49
) and (
5.56
)–(
5.58
), we can deduce that

\int_{0}^{T}\|\mu(t)-\overline{\mu(t)}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{4}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq CT+C\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|\nabla\partial_{t}(t)\varphi\|^{2}+\|\nabla\bm{v}(t)\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C,

that is,
\mu\in L^{4}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))
for any
T>0
.

Consider the elliptic problem (
5.25
). Using (
5.22
), (
5.35
), (
5.56
), (
5.57
) and (H5), we can apply the same argument as in
[
31
, Section 3]
to conclude (
5.44
).
This strict separation property holds for all
t\in[0,T]
, because of the continuity
\varphi\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\Omega}))
due to (
5.55
), (
5.57
), the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma and the Sobolev embedding theorem.
Moreover, it enables us to gain more regularity of the strong solution. Since
\Psi\in C^{2}([-1+\delta_{1},\,1-\delta_{1}])
, we can deduce from (
5.57
) that
\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
. Applying the classical elliptic estimate to the elliptic equation
\eqref{CH}_{2}
, we further get

(5.59)

\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega)).

The proof of Proposition
5.4
is complete.
∎

5.3.
Inhomogeneous Stokes system with forcing

Let
\bm{u}
,
\varphi
,
\mu
,
\sigma
be four given functions with suitable regularity properties. We consider the following Stokes system with a forcing term (cf. Remark
2.2
)

(5.60)

\begin{cases}\rho(\varphi)\partial_{t}\bm{v}+\big((\rho(\varphi)\bm{u}+\mathbf{J})\cdot\nabla\big)\bm{v}-\mathrm{div}\big(2\nu(\varphi)D\bm{v}\big)+\nabla P=\big(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma\big)\nabla\varphi,\\
\qquad\text{with}\ \ \mathbf{J}=-\rho^{\prime}(\varphi)m(\varphi)\nabla\mu,\quad\rho(\varphi)=\widetilde{\rho}_{1}\dfrac{1-\varphi}{2}+\widetilde{\rho}_{2}\dfrac{1+\varphi}{2},\\
\mathrm{div}\,\bm{v}=0,\end{cases}

in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, subject to the boundary and initial conditions

(5.61)

\begin{cases}\bm{v}=\mathbf{0},\quad&\text{on }\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty),\\
\bm{v}|_{t=0}=\bm{v}_{0},\quad&\text{in }\Omega.\end{cases}

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with a
C^{3}
boundary. Suppose that assumptions (H1)–(H4) are satisfied. Let

\displaystyle\bm{u}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{2}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\Omega)).

Moreover, we assume that
(\varphi,\mu)
is determined by Proposition
5.4
with the functions
(\bm{u},\sigma)
given above as drift and conservative forcing terms.

(1) For any initial datum
\bm{v}_{0}\in\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
, problem (
5.60
)–(
5.61
) admits a global weak solution
\bm{v}
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which satisfies

\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),\quad\bm{P}\partial_{t}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{v})\in L^{2}(0,T;(\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))^{\prime}),

for any
T>0
. The solution
\bm{v}
satisfies

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{v}),\bm{\zeta}\rangle_{(\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))^{\prime},\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))}-(\rho(\varphi)\bm{u}\otimes\bm{v},\nabla\bm{\zeta})-(\bm{v}\otimes\mathbf{J},\nabla\bm{\zeta})+\big(2\nu(\varphi)D\bm{v},D\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\big((\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\nabla\varphi,\bm{\zeta}\big),\quad\forall\,\bm{\zeta}\in\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega),

almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, the boundary condition
\bm{v}=\mathbf{0}
almost everywhere on
\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty)
and the initial condition
\bm{v}|_{t=0}=\bm{v}_{0}

almost everywhere in
\Omega
.

(2) For any initial datum
\bm{v}_{0}\in\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)
, problem (
5.60
)–(
5.61
) admits a unique global strong solution
\bm{v}
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which satisfies

\bm{v}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,T;\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}(0,T;\bm{L}_{\sigma}^{2}(\Omega)),

for any
T>0
.
The solution
\bm{v}
satisfies the system (
5.60
) almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, the boundary condition
\bm{v}=\mathbf{0}
almost everywhere on
\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty)
and the initial condition
\bm{v}|_{t=0}=\bm{v}_{0}
almost everywhere in
\Omega
.

Proof.

Existence.
The proof for the existence part is based on a Faedo–Galerkin approximation scheme similar to that in
[
37
]
. In the following, we only perform formal
a priori
estimates.

Testing the first equation in (
5.60
) by
\bm{v}
, using (
5.15
) and integration by parts, we get

(5.62)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi)|\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi)|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x=-\int_{\Omega}\varphi\nabla(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\cdot\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x.

In view of (
5.22
), it holds

\displaystyle\left|-\int_{\Omega}\varphi\nabla(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\cdot\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\|)\|\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)})\|\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+C(\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}),

where the constant
C>0
depends on
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}
.
Thus, an application of Gronwall’s lemma to (
5.62
) yields

(5.63)

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}(t)\|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\bm{v}(s)\|^{2}\,{\rm d}s\leq C,\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

where
C>0
depends on
\|\bm{v}_{0}\|
,
T
,
\nu_{*}
,
\rho_{*}
,
\rho^{*}
,
\|\mu\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
.
Besides, from (
5.60
) and the regularity properties of
(\bm{u},\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
, we can verify that

(5.64)

\displaystyle\|\bm{P}\partial_{t}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{v})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;(\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))^{\prime})}\leq C.

We proceed to derive higher-order estimates. Testing (
5.60
) by
\partial_{t}\bm{v}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\varphi)|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi)|\partial_{t}\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{u}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}(\mathbf{J}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x

(5.65)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{\Omega}\nu^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\varphi\nabla(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x.

Using the Ladyzhenskaya inequality and Korn’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\varphi)\bm{u}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{*}\|\bm{u}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{u}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{u}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho_{*}}{8}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|^{2}+\eta\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C_{\eta}\|\nabla\bm{u}\|^{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}.

In a similar manner, we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\mathbf{J}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|m(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mu\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho_{*}}{8}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|^{2}+\eta\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C_{\eta}\|\mu\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|2\int_{\Omega}\nu^{\prime}(\varphi)\partial_{t}\varphi|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nu^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|D\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|D\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C_{\eta}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\varphi\nabla(\mu-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{v}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\|)\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)})\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho_{*}}{8}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|^{2}+C(\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}).

Here, the small constant
\eta\in(0,1)
will be determined later.
Exploiting the regularity theory of the Stokes equation with concentration-dependent viscosity
(see, e.g.,
[
1
, Lemma 4]
) and using Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|+C\|\rho(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{u}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\rho^{\prime}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|m(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla\mu\|+\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi)\sigma)\|),

which combined with the Ladyzhenskaya inequality and Young’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\bm{u}\|^{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}+C\|\mu\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\bm{v}\|^{2}

(5.66)

\displaystyle\quad+C(\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}).

Combining the above estimates and taking
\eta>0
sufficiently small, we can deduce from
(
5.65
) that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\nu(\varphi)|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\frac{\rho_{*}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\|\nabla\bm{u}\|^{2}+\|\mu\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi\|^{2})\int_{\Omega}\nu(\varphi)|D\bm{v}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

(5.67)

\displaystyle\qquad+C(\|\nabla\mu\|^{2}+\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}).

Then, by Gronwall’s lemma and (
5.66
), we conclude that

(5.68)

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}(t)\|_{\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}(s)\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}(s)\|^{2}_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}\big)\,{\rm d}s\leq C,\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T],

where
C>0
depends on
\|\nabla\bm{v}_{0}\|
,
T
,
\rho_{*}
,
\rho^{*}
,
\nu_{*}
,
\nu^{*}
,
\|\mu\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,

\|\mu\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}
,
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Omega))}
,

\|\partial_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,

\|\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
.

The above estimates enable us to construct a weak as well as a strong solution on
[0,\infty)
.
On the other hand, uniqueness of the strong solution is a consequence of the following weak-strong uniqueness result.

Weak-strong uniqueness.
Assume that
\bm{v}_{1}
(resp.
\bm{v}_{2}
) is a weak (resp. strong) solution to problem (
5.60
)–(
5.61
) with the given data
(\bm{u},\varphi,\mu,\sigma)
, and both solutions satisfy the same initial data
\bm{v}_{0}
. Let
T>0
. It is straightforward to check that
\bm{v}_{1}
satisfies the energy inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi(t))|\bm{v}_{1}(t)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi(s))|D\bm{v}_{1}(s)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi(0))|\bm{v}_{0}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(\mu(s)-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi(s))\sigma(s))\nabla\varphi(s)\cdot\bm{v}_{1}(s)\,{\rm d}x\mathrm{d}s,

while
\bm{v}_{2}
satisfies the energy equality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi(t))|\bm{v}_{2}(t)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi(s))|D\bm{v}_{2}(s)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi(0))|\bm{v}_{0}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(\mu(s)-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi(s))\sigma(s))\nabla\varphi(s)\cdot\bm{v}_{2}(s)\,{\rm d}x\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T]
.
On the other hand, the regularity properties of
\bm{v}_{1}
and
\bm{v}_{2}
allow us to take
\bm{\zeta}=\bm{v}_{2}
in the weak formulation of
\bm{v}_{1}
and test the equation for
\bm{v}_{2}
by
\bm{v}_{1}
. Taking these facts into account, following the same argument as in the proof of
[
5
, Theorem 4.1]
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\varphi(t))|\bm{v}_{1}(t)-\bm{v}_{2}(t)|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi(s))|D(\bm{v}_{1}(s)-\bm{v}_{2}(s))|^{2}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}s\leq 0,\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T].

Hence, it easily follows that
\bm{v}_{1}(t)=\bm{v}_{2}(t)
on
[0,T]
.

The proof of Proposition
5.5
is complete.
∎

6.
Existence and Uniqueness of a Global Strong Solution

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2.2
on the existence and uniqueness of a global strong solution.

6.1.
Existence

Under the assumption of Theorem
2.2
, problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) admits a global weak solution
(\bm{v}^{*},\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})
in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
thanks to Theorem
2.1
-(2). Our aim is to prove that this weak solution is actually a strong one.

The proof is based on a bootstrap-type argument.

Step 1.
Applying Proposition
5.2
, we find that problem (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) with
\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{*}
and the same initial datum
\sigma_{0}
admits a unique global strong solution in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which is denoted by
\sigma^{\natural}
. Since
\sigma^{*}
is a global weak solution to the same problem, then by the uniqueness of weak solutions due to Proposition
5.1
, we have
\sigma^{*}=\sigma^{\natural}
on
[0,\infty)
, noticing that
T>0
is arbitrary therein.

Next, from the facts

\sigma^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\operatorname{uloc}}([0,\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)),\quad\varphi^{*}\in L_{\operatorname{uloc}}^{2}([0,\infty),W^{2,q}(\Omega)),

for any
q\in[2,\infty)
, we can apply (
5.11
), the uniform Gronwall lemma combined with Gronwall’s lemma to obtain the improved estimate (cf. (
5.12
))

(6.1)

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{\natural}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\sigma^{\natural}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

which further yields
\sigma^{\natural}\in H^{1}_{\operatorname{uloc}}([0,\infty);L^{2}(\Omega))
.
Here, the property
\sigma^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\Omega))
is crucial, as it guarantees that the constant
C
does not depend on time.

Step 2.
Applying Proposition
5.4
, we find that problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
) with
\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\sigma=\sigma^{*}
(keeping in mind that now
\sigma^{*}=\sigma^{\natural}
, i.e., a strong one) and the initial datum
\varphi_{0}
admits a unique global strong solution in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which is denoted by
(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural})
.
Since
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})
is a global weak solution to the same problem, by the uniqueness of weak solutions due to Proposition
5.3
, we have
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})=(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural})
on
[0,\infty)
.

With the aid of the uniform in time estimates for
\bm{v}^{*}
,
\varphi^{*}
,
\mu^{*}
and the estimate (
6.1
) obtained in the previous step, we can use (
5.51
), in which the positive constant
C
is now independent of time, the uniform Gronwall lemma combined with Gronwall’s lemma to obtain the following improved estimates (cf. (
5.55
))

(6.2)

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mu^{\natural}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\varphi^{\natural}\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,\end{split}

which further imply

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\natural}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\mu^{\natural}\|_{L^{4}(t,t+1;H^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C,

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{\natural}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\|\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi^{\natural})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq C,

for any
q\in[2,\infty)
. With the above uniform-in-time estimates, we can find a constant
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)
independent of time such that (cf. (
5.44
))

\|\varphi^{\natural}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1-\delta_{1},\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

As a consequence, this gives (cf. (
5.59
))

(6.5)

\|\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi^{\natural})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,\quad\|\varphi^{\natural}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{3}(\Omega))}\leq C.

Moreover, if
\sigma_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, using (
6.4
) and Corollary
5.1
, we can further obtain

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{\natural}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C,\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Step 3.
From Proposition
5.5
-(2), we find that problem (
5.60
)–(
5.61
) with
\bm{u}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{*}
,
\mu=\mu^{*}
,
\sigma=\sigma^{*}
(note that
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})=(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural},\sigma^{\natural})
is actually a strong solution satisfying the improved estimates obtained above) and the initial datum
\bm{v}_{0}
admits a unique global strong solution in
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
, which is denoted by
\bm{v}^{\natural}
. Since
\bm{v}^{*}
is a global weak solution to the same problem, by the weak-strong uniqueness result shown in the proof of Proposition
5.5
, we find
\bm{v}^{*}=\bm{v}^{\natural}
on
[0,\infty)
. Moreover, using the estimates (
6.2
), (
6.3
), (
6.4
), we can apply (
5.67
), in which the positive constant
C
is now independent of time, the uniform Gronwall lemma combined with Gronwall’s lemma to obtain (cf. (
5.68
))

(6.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\bm{v}^{\natural}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C,\\
&\sup_{t\geq 0}\,(\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{\natural}(t)\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\bm{v}^{\natural}(t)\|_{L^{2}(t,t+1;\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega))})\leq C.\end{split}

In summary, we have shown that
(\bm{v}^{*},\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})=(\bm{v}^{\natural},\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural},\sigma^{\natural})
on
[0,\infty)
.
By the construction of
(\bm{v}^{\natural},\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural},\sigma^{\natural})
,
(\bm{v}^{*},\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})
is indeed a global strong solution in the sense of Definition
2.2
. Hence, this establishes the existence of a global strong solution to problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) on the whole interval
[0,\infty)
.

\square

6.2.
Uniqueness

Let
(\bm{v}_{i},\varphi_{i},\mu_{i},\sigma_{i})
,
i=1,2
, be two global strong solutions to problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) subject to the initial data
(\bm{v}_{0,i},\varphi_{0,i},\sigma_{0,i})
. We denote the differences

\displaystyle\bm{V}=\bm{v}_{1}-\bm{v}_{2},\quad P=P_{1}-P_{2},\quad\Phi=\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2},\quad\Upsilon=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},\quad\Sigma=\sigma_{1}-\sigma_{2},

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{0}=\bm{v}_{0,1}-\bm{v}_{0,2},\quad\Phi_{0}=\varphi_{0,1}-\varphi_{0,2},\quad\Sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0,1}-\sigma_{0,2}.

Then it holds

(6.8)

\begin{cases}\rho(\varphi_{1})\partial_{t}\bm{V}+(\rho(\varphi_{1})-\rho(\varphi_{2}))\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}+\big(\rho(\varphi_{1})(\bm{v}_{1}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{1}-\rho(\varphi_{2})(\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\big)\\
\qquad-\big(\rho^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})m(\varphi_{1})(\nabla\mu_{1}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{1}-\rho^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})m(\varphi_{2})(\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\big)-\mathrm{div}\big(2\nu(\varphi_{1})D\bm{V}\big)\\
\qquad-\mathrm{div}\big(2(\nu(\varphi_{1})-\nu(\varphi_{2}))D\bm{v}_{2}\big)+\nabla\widetilde{P}\\
\quad=-\mathrm{div}(\nabla\varphi_{1}\otimes\nabla\Phi+\nabla\Phi\otimes\nabla\varphi_{2})-\big(\sigma_{1}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{1})-\sigma_{2}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{2})\big),\\
\mathrm{div}\,\bm{V}=0,\\
\partial_{t}\Phi+\bm{v}_{1}\cdot\nabla\Phi+\bm{V}\cdot\nabla\varphi_{2}=\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi_{1})\nabla\mu_{1}\big)-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi_{2})\nabla\mu_{2}\big),\\
\Upsilon=-\Delta\Phi+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})+\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\sigma_{1}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\sigma_{2},\\
\partial_{t}\Sigma+\bm{v}_{1}\cdot\nabla\Sigma+\bm{V}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{2}-\Delta\Sigma=\mathrm{div}\left(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\sigma_{1}\nabla\varphi_{1}\right)-\mathrm{div}\left(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\sigma_{2}\nabla\varphi_{2}\right),\end{cases}

almost everywhere in
\Omega\times(0,\infty)
, subject to the boundary and initial conditions

(6.9)

\begin{cases}\bm{V}=\mathbf{0},\quad\partial_{\bm{n}}\Phi=\partial_{\bm{n}}\Upsilon=\partial_{\bm{n}}\Sigma=0\quad&\text{a.e. on }\partial\Omega\times(0,\infty),\\
\bm{V}|_{t=0}=\bm{V}_{0},\quad\Phi|_{t=0}=\Phi_{0},\quad\Sigma|_{t=0}=\Sigma_{0}\quad&\text{a.e. in }\Omega.\end{cases}

In (
6.8
)
1
, we have denoted the modified pressure by

\widetilde{P}=P-\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi_{1}|^{2}+\Psi(\varphi_{1})\right)+\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\varphi_{2}|^{2}+\Psi(\varphi_{2})\right).

Testing (
6.8
)
1
by
\bm{V}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})|\bm{V}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+2\int_{\Omega}\nu(\varphi_{1})|D\bm{V}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\varphi_{1})-\rho(\varphi_{2}))\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})(\bm{V}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\varphi_{1})-\rho(\varphi_{2}))(\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}\big((\rho(\varphi_{1})m(\varphi_{1})\nabla\mu_{1}-\rho(\varphi_{2})m(\varphi_{2})\nabla\mu_{2})\cdot\nabla\big)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int_{\Omega}(\nu(\varphi_{1})-\nu(\varphi_{2}))D\bm{v}_{2}:\nabla\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\nabla\varphi_{1}\otimes\nabla\Phi+\nabla\Phi\otimes\nabla\varphi_{2}):\nabla\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\Omega}\big(\sigma_{1}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{1})-\sigma_{2}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{2}))\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

(6.10)

\displaystyle\quad=:\sum_{i=1}^{7}Z_{i}.

The terms
Z_{i}
,
i=1,2,3,5,6
can be estimated exactly the same as in
[
37
, Section 6]
, thus we obtain

\displaystyle|Z_{1}|+|Z_{2}|+|Z_{3}|+|Z_{5}|+|Z_{6}|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{6}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+C\big(\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}_{2}\|^{2}_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}\big)\big(\|\bm{V}\|^{2}+\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\big).

Concerning
Z_{4}
, we find

\displaystyle Z_{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})m(\varphi_{1})(\nabla\Upsilon\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})(m(\varphi_{1})-m(\varphi_{2}))(\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\varphi_{1})-\rho(\varphi_{2}))m(\varphi_{2})(\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}_{2}\cdot\bm{V}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=:Z_{4}^{(1)}+Z_{4}^{(2)}+Z_{4}^{(3)}.

We note that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Upsilon\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|+\|\nabla(\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\|+\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\sigma_{1}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\sigma_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|+\|\Psi^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Phi\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\Psi^{\prime\prime\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Sigma\|+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{\prime\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{1}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\Sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}+\|\beta^{\prime\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Phi\|\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{\prime\prime\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|+C\Big(1+\|\sigma_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+C_{*}\|\nabla\Sigma\|+C\|\Sigma\|,

where

C_{*}=\max_{r\in[-1,1]}|\beta^{\prime}(r)|+1,

and the positive constant
C
depends on the strict separation property of
\varphi_{1}
,
\varphi_{2}
on
[0,T]
,
\|\varphi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega))}
,
\|\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
.
Hence, it holds

\displaystyle|Z_{4}^{(1)}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|m(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Upsilon\|\|\nabla\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\Upsilon\|\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{6}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\bm{V}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(1+\|\sigma_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\big)\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|^{2},

where
C>0
also depends on
\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\bm{H}^{1}(\Omega))}
.
Next, we infer from Agmon’s inequality that

\displaystyle|Z_{4}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\int_{0}^{1}m^{\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu_{2}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{\frac{1}{4}}+\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\big)\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{6}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\bm{V}\|^{2}+C\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2},

and

\displaystyle|Z_{4}^{(3)}|

\displaystyle\leq\|m(\varphi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu_{2}\|\|\nabla\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{6}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+C\|\bm{v}_{2}\|_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\bm{V}\|^{2}+C\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}.

For
Z_{7}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|Z_{7}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{1}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\Sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Phi\|\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{\prime\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{2}\|\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{V}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}+C\|\bm{V}\|^{2}+C\|\sigma_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|^{2}.

Testing the third equation in (
6.8
) by
-\Delta\Phi
, after integration by parts, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|\nabla\Phi\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi_{1})|\nabla\Delta\Phi|^{2}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla(\bm{v}_{1}\cdot\nabla\Phi)\cdot\nabla\Phi\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\Omega}\nabla(\bm{V}\cdot\nabla\varphi_{2})\cdot\nabla\Phi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi_{1})\nabla(\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\cdot\nabla\Delta\Phi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi_{1})\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\sigma_{1}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\sigma_{2})\cdot\nabla\Delta\Phi\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\Omega}(m(\varphi_{1})-m(\varphi_{2}))\nabla\mu_{2}\cdot\nabla\Delta\Phi\,{\rm d}x

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad=:\sum_{i=8}^{12}Z_{i}.

We recall that
Z_{8}
,
Z_{9}
can be estimated in the same way as in
[
37
, Section 6]
such that

\displaystyle|Z_{8}|+|Z_{9}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{6}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+C\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Using a similar argument for (
6.11
) together with Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|Z_{10}|+|Z_{11}|

\displaystyle\leq\|m(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\big(\|\nabla(\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})-\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi_{2}))\|+\|\nabla(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\sigma_{1}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{2})\sigma_{2})\|\big)\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+C_{**}\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}+C\big(1+\|\sigma_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\big)\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|^{2},

where

C_{**}=\frac{8(m^{*}C_{*})^{2}}{m_{*}}.

Concerning
Z_{12}
, it holds

\displaystyle|Z_{12}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{1}m^{\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mu_{2}\|\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{*}}{12}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+C\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Testing the fifth equation in (
6.8
) with
\Sigma
, after integration by parts, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\Sigma\|^{2}+\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}(\bm{V}\cdot\nabla\sigma_{2})\Sigma\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\big(\sigma_{1}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{1})-\sigma_{2}\nabla\beta(\varphi_{2})\big)\cdot\nabla\Sigma\,{\rm d}x

(6.13)

\displaystyle=:Z_{13}+Z_{14}.

We observe that

\displaystyle|Z_{13}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{V}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\sigma_{2}\|\|\Sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\bm{V}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Sigma\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Sigma\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{*}}{24(1+C_{**})}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}+C\|\bm{V}\|^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|^{2},

and

\displaystyle|Z_{14}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{1}\|_{\bm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\|\Sigma\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Sigma\|+\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi_{1})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Phi\|\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Sigma\|

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{\prime\prime}(s\varphi_{1}+(1-s)\varphi_{2})\Phi\,{\rm d}s\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\varphi_{2}\|\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\Sigma\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}+C\|\sigma_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\Phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|^{2}.

Collecting the above estimates, multiplying (
6.13
) by
4(1+C_{**})
and adding the resultant with (
6.10
), (
6.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left(\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})|\bm{V}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\|\nabla\Phi\|^{2}+|\overline{\Phi}|^{2}+4(1+C_{**})\|\Sigma\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\nu_{*}\|D\bm{V}\|^{2}+m_{*}\|\nabla\Delta\Phi\|^{2}+2(1+C_{**})\|\nabla\Sigma\|^{2}

(6.14)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\mathcal{F}(t)\left(\int_{\Omega}\rho(\varphi_{1})|\bm{V}|^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\|\nabla\Phi\|^{2}+|\overline{\Phi}|^{2}+4(1+C_{**})\|\Sigma\|^{2}\right),

for almost all
t\in(0,T)
, where

\mathcal{F}(t)=1+\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}_{2}(t)\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}_{2}(t)\|^{2}_{\bm{H}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\sigma_{2}(t)\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

We note that the constant
C>0
in (
6.14
) depends on the strict separation property of
\varphi_{1}
,
\varphi_{2}
on
[0,T]
,
\|\varphi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega))}
,
\|\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3}(\Omega))}
,
\|\sigma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}
,
\rho_{*}
,
\rho^{*}
,
\nu_{*}
,
m_{*}
,
m^{*}
,
\beta^{*}
,
C_{*}
,
\Omega
and
T
. In the above derivation, we have also used the mass conservation
\overline{\Phi(t)}=\overline{\Phi_{0}}
and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality. Since
\mathcal{F}\in L^{1}(0,T)
, if

(\bm{v}_{0,1},\varphi_{0,1},\sigma_{0,1})=(\bm{v}_{0,2},\varphi_{0,2},\sigma_{0,2})
, the conclusion
