(\bm{v}_{1},\varphi_{1},\sigma_{1})=(\bm{v}_{2},\varphi_{2},\sigma_{2})
on
[0,T]
is a direct consequence of Gronwall’s lemma. Hence, the global strong solution to problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) is unique.

The proof of Theorem
2.2
is complete.

\square

7.
Propagation of Regularity for Global Weak Solutions

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.3
on the propagation of regularity for global weak solutions.

The proof is based on a bootstrap-type argument in the sprit of Section
6.1
. Let
(\bm{v}^{*},\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})
be a global weak solution given by Theorem
2.1
-(2). Assume that
\tau\in(0,1)
is an arbitrary but fixed time.

Step 1. Global regularity of
\sigma^{*}
.

Since
\sigma^{*}\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega))
, there exists
\tau_{1}\in(0,\tau)
such that
\sigma^{*}(\tau_{1})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
with
\sigma^{*}(\tau_{1})\geq 0
almost everywhere in
\Omega
. At the same time, we have the regularity properties

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\bm{L}^{2}_{\sigma}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,\infty;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([0,\infty);W^{2,q}(\Omega)),

for any
q\geq 2
.
Hence, taking
\sigma^{*}(\tau_{1})
as the new initial datum, we infer from Proposition
5.2
and the argument in Section
6.1
that problem
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
) with
\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{*}
admits a unique global strong solution denoted by
\sigma^{\natural}
on
[\tau_{1},\infty)
such that

(7.1)

\begin{split}\sigma^{\natural}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{1},\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([\tau_{1},\infty);H^{2}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([\tau_{1},\infty);L^{2}(\Omega)).\end{split}

Since
\sigma^{*}
is a global weak solution to the same problem on
[\tau_{1},\infty)
, then by the uniqueness of weak solutions due to Proposition
5.1
, we find
\sigma^{*}=\sigma^{\natural}
, which establishes the regularization of
\sigma^{*}
for
t\geq\tau_{1}
.

Step 2. Global regularity of
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})
.

Since

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,1;H_{N}^{2}(\Omega)),\quad\mu^{*}\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\in L^{\infty}(0,1;L^{2}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega)),

then applying (
5.22
) and (
5.26
) to
(\varphi^{*},\sigma^{*})
, we find
\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{*})\sigma^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega))}
is bounded as well. As a result, it holds
-\Delta\varphi^{*}+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi^{*})\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(\Omega))
. Hence, there exist
\tau_{2}\in(\tau_{1},\tau)
such that
\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})\in H^{2}_{N}(\Omega)
with

\|\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1
,
\big|\overline{\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})}\big|<1
,
\mu^{*}_{\tau_{2}}:=-\Delta\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})+\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2}))\in H^{1}(\Omega)
and
\sigma^{*}(\tau_{2})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
.

Consider problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
), with
\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})
being the new initial datum and
\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\sigma=\sigma^{*}
. Thanks to the fact
\sigma^{*}=\sigma^{\natural}
, the regularity (
7.1
) and the Sobolev embedding theorem, we can conclude that

\sigma^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;L^{q}(\Omega))\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([\tau_{2},\infty);L^{2}(\Omega)),

for any
q\geq 2
.
Applying Proposition
5.4
and the argument in Section
6.1
, we see that problem (
5.15
)–(
5.16
) admits a unique strong solution denoted by
(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural})
on
[\tau_{2},\infty)
such that

(7.2)

\begin{split}&\varphi^{\natural}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega)),\quad\partial_{t}\varphi^{\natural}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([\tau_{2},\infty);H^{1}(\Omega)),\\
&\mu^{\natural}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}_{\mathrm{uloc}}([\tau_{2},\infty);H^{3}(\Omega)),\quad\Psi^{\prime}(\varphi^{\natural})\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;L^{q}(\Omega)),\end{split}

for any
q\geq 2
. Since
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})
is a global weak solution to the same problem on
[\tau_{2},\infty)
, then by the uniqueness of weak solutions due to Proposition
5.3
, we find
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})=(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural})
on
[\tau_{2},\infty)
, which establishes the regularization of
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*})
for
t\geq\tau_{2}
.

Next, we establish the strict separation property of
\varphi^{*}
. At the initial time
\tau_{2}
, since
\mu^{*}({\tau_{2}})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
and
\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2}))\sigma^{*}(\tau_{2})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
(owing to the facts
\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})\in H^{2}(\Omega)
,
\sigma^{*}(\tau_{2})\in H^{1}(\Omega)
), by
[
39
, Lemma 7.4]
, we find
\|\Psi_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2}))\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq C(q)
for any
q\in[2,\infty)
.
Then applying
[
46
, Lemma 3.2]
, we can conclude that
\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2}))\in W^{1,q}(\Omega)
for any
q\in[2,\infty)
, which further implies
\Psi_{0}^{\prime}(\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2}))\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
. Hence, there exists a constant
\delta(\tau_{2})\in(0,1)
such that

(7.3)

\|\varphi^{*}(\tau_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1-\delta(\tau_{2}).

By the same reasoning, we deduce from the facts

\varphi^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;W^{2,q}(\Omega)),\quad\mu^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;H^{1}(\Omega)),\quad\sigma^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;H^{1}(\Omega))

that
there exists a constant
\delta_{2}(\tau_{2})\in(0,\delta(\tau_{2})]
such that

(7.4)

\|\varphi^{*}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 1-\delta_{2}(\tau_{2}),\quad\forall\,t\geq\tau_{2}.

Finally, the strict separation property (
7.4
) combined with the elliptic estimate for (
5.15
)
2
further implies that
\varphi^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\tau_{2},\infty;H^{3}(\Omega))
.

Step 3. Global regularity of
\bm{v}^{*}
.

Since
\bm{v}^{*}\in L^{2}(0,1;\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega))
, there exists some
\tau_{3}\in(\tau_{2},\tau)
such that
\bm{v}(\tau_{3})\in\bm{H}^{1}_{0,\sigma}(\Omega)
. From Proposition
5.5
-(2), we find that problem (
5.60
)–(
5.61
) with
\bm{u}=\bm{v}^{*}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{*}
,
\mu=\mu^{*}
,
\sigma=\sigma^{*}
(note that
(\varphi^{*},\mu^{*},\sigma^{*})=(\varphi^{\natural},\mu^{\natural},\sigma^{\natural})
is actually a strong solution that satisfies the improved estimates obtained above) and the initial datum
\bm{v}(\tau_{3})
admits a unique global strong solution on
[\tau_{3},\infty)
, which is denoted by
\bm{v}^{\natural}
. Since
\bm{v}^{*}
is a global weak solution to the same problem, then by the weak-strong uniqueness result again, we find
\bm{v}^{*}=\bm{v}^{\natural}
on
[\tau_{3},\infty)
.

Since
\tau\in(0,1)
is arbitrary, we can conclude that every global weak solution to problem (
2.8
)–(
2.9
) becomes a global strong one for
t>0
.

The proof of Theorem
2.3
is complete.

\square

Appendix A
Appendix

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.2

In the appendix, we sketch the proof of Lemma
3.2
.

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

In view of Equation (
3.32
), we find that
\mu^{k}
(i.e.,
\{c_{i}^{k}\}_{i=1}^{k}
) can be uniquely determined by
(\varphi^{k},\sigma^{k})
. Hence, problem (
3.30
)–(
3.35
) can be reduced to a system with
2k
nonlinear ordinary differential equations for the time-dependent coefficients
\{a_{i}^{k}\}_{i=1}^{k}
(by taking
\bm{\zeta}=\bm{y}_{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,k
) and
\{b_{i}^{k}\}_{i=1}^{k}
(by taking
w=z_{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,k
). Since
\widehat{\rho}
is bounded with a strictly positive lower bound, then the Gram matrix
\{(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{y}_{i},\bm{y}_{j})\}_{i,j=1,\cdots,k}
in the first term on the left-hand side of (
3.30
) is non-degenerate. From the assumptions (H2)–(H4), the construction of
\Psi_{\epsilon}
and the regularity property of
\sigma^{k}
, we can apply the Cauchy–Lipschitz theorem for nonlinear ODE systems to conclude the existence and uniqueness of local solutions
\{a_{i}^{k}\}_{i=1}^{k}
,
\{b_{i}^{k}\}_{i=1}^{k}\subset C^{1}([0,T_{k}])
, which satisfy the above mentioned ODE system on a certain time interval
[0,T_{k}]\subset[0,T]
.
Therefore, we obtain a unique local solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k},\mu^{k})
to problem (
3.30
)–(
3.35
) that satisfies

\bm{v}^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T_{k}];\bm{Y}_{k}),\quad\varphi^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T_{k}];Z_{k}),\quad\mu^{k}\in C^{1}([0,T_{k}];Z_{k}).

We proceed to show that the existence time
T_{k}
can be extended to
T
.
Testing (
3.30
) by
\bm{v}^{k}
, using the incompressibility condition and integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big)+\int_{\Omega}2\nu(\varphi^{k})|D\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\gamma\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.1)

\displaystyle\quad=\big(\mu^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big)-\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big),

for any
t\in(0,T_{k})
. Next, testing (
3.31
) by
\mu^{k}
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\,\mathrm{d}x\right)+\int_{\Omega}m(\varphi^{k})|\nabla\mu^{k}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.2)

\displaystyle\quad=-\big(\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k},\mu^{k}\big)-\big(\partial_{t}\beta(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\big),

for any
t\in(0,T_{k})
.
We note that the first terms on the right-hand side of (
A.1
) and (
A.2
) cancel each other.
By Hölder’s inequality and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality, we find

\displaystyle\left|\big(\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|_{\bm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\sigma^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}\|_{H^{3}(\Omega)}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|

(A.3)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\|\sigma^{k}\|\big(\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}^{k}\|^{2}+1\big).

Concerning the second term on the right-hand side of (
A.2
), we take

\xi=\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}
in (
3.31
) and apply the same argument as in
[
40
, Appendix 2]
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}\|\leq C_{k}(\|\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}\|+\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|).

Then, by Young’s inequality and the assumption (H4), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\beta(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}\|\|\sigma^{k}\|

(A.4)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{*}}{2}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}+C_{k}\|\bm{v}^{k}\|\|\varphi^{k}\|\|\sigma^{k}\|.

Adding (
A.1
) with (
A.2
), we infer from (
A.3
), (
A.4
) and the Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\,\mathrm{d}x\right)+2\nu_{*}\int_{\Omega}|D\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{m_{*}}{2}\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}

(A.5)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{1,k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2})\left(\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k},\bm{v}^{k}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\,\mathrm{d}x\right)+C_{1,k}(1+\|\sigma^{k}\|^{2}).

Moreover, the initial value of the energy can be controlled as follows

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left[\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\rho}(\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0,\gamma})|\bm{P}_{\bm{Y}_{k}}\bm{v}_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0}|^{2}+\Psi_{\epsilon}(\bm{P}_{Z_{k}}\varphi_{0})\right]\mathrm{d}x

(A.6)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\Big(\rho^{*},\|\bm{v}_{0}\|,\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)},\max_{r\in[-1,1]}|\Psi_{0}(r)|,\Omega\Big)=:C_{0}.

Exploiting the convexity of
\Psi_{0,\epsilon}
, we have

(A.7)

\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi^{k}\|^{2}+\int_{\Omega}\Psi_{\epsilon}(\varphi^{k})\,\mathrm{d}x\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\varphi^{k}\|^{2}-C_{0,k},

for some
C_{0,k}\geq 0
.
Hence, applying Gronwall’s lemma to (
A.5
), we deduce from (
A.7
) that

(A.8)

\displaystyle\rho_{*}\|\bm{v}^{k}(t)\|^{2}+\|\varphi^{k}(t)\|^{2}\leq 2\mathrm{e}^{N_{1}(t)}\big(C_{0}+N_{1}(t)\big)+2C_{0,k}=:N_{2}(t),\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{k}],

with

\displaystyle N_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=tC_{1,k}\Big(1+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\sigma^{k}(t)\|^{2}\Big).

Thanks to (
A.8
), a further integration in time of (
A.5
) gives

\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0}+C_{0,k}+N_{1}(t)N_{2}(t)+N_{1}(t)=:N_{3}(t),\quad\forall\,t\in[0,T_{k}].

Based on the above estimates and Lemma
3.1
,
we can extend the unique local solution
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
to the whole interval
[0,T]
,
with the same estimate as (
A.8
).
Using (
3.21
), we can rewrite (
3.30
) as

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big(((\widehat{\rho}(\varphi^{k})\bm{v}^{k}+\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{k})\cdot\nabla)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)+\big(2\nu(\varphi^{k})D\bm{v}^{k},D\bm{\zeta}\big)+\gamma(|\nabla\bm{v}^{k}|^{2}\nabla\bm{v}^{k},\nabla\bm{\zeta}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\big((\mu^{k}-\beta^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})\sigma^{k})\nabla\varphi^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{R}^{k}\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big)

(A.9)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\rho}^{\prime}(\varphi^{k})(\bm{I}-\bm{P}_{Z_{k}})\big((\bm{v}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k})-\mathrm{div}\big(m(\varphi^{k})\nabla\mu^{k}\big)\big)\bm{v}^{k},\bm{\zeta}\big).

Testing (
A.9
) by
\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{k}
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{k}\max\{\rho^{*},1\}}{\rho_{*}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{4}+\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}+\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{6}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{k}\max\{\rho^{*},1\}}{\rho_{*}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\big(\|\nabla\mu^{k}\|^{2}+\|\sigma^{k}(s)\|^{2}\big)\|\varphi^{k}(s)\|^{2}+\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\varphi^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C_{k}\max\{\rho^{*},1\}}{\rho_{*}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\varphi^{k}(s)\|^{2}+\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{k}\left(TN^{2}_{2}(T)+N_{3}(T)N_{2}(T)+TN_{2}(T)+TN^{3}_{2}(T)+TN_{2}(T)\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\sigma^{k}(t)\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{k}\left(N_{3}(T)N_{2}(T)^{2}+N_{2}(T)^{2}+N_{3}(T)N_{2}(T)\right).

In a similar manner, we can conclude from (
3.31
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\varphi^{k}(s)\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|\bm{v}^{k}(s)\|^{2}\|\varphi^{k}(s)\|^{2}+\|\nabla\mu^{k}(s)\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}\big(TN^{2}_{2}(T)+N_{3}(T)\big).

Hence,
(\bm{v}^{k},\varphi^{k})
is bounded in
H^{1}(0,T;\bm{Y}_{k})\times H^{1}(0,T;Z_{k})
.

The proof of Lemma
3.2
is complete.
∎
